```yaml
  - ὀλίγου
  - ἐν ὀλίγῳ χρόνῳ
english_gloss: small coin, for a little, in a little time
observation: The Eleatic Stranger notes that the sophist sells each product of his universal production for a very small coin, and that he claims to teach everything for a little fee and in a short time.
textual_basis: At 234a the Stranger says the sophist sells each thing for a very small coin. At 234a-234b he describes one who claims to know and teach everything for a little fee and in a little time.
limits: The observation records the fee-for-instruction motif applied to the sophist. It does not assess whether this price claim is historically accurate for any named sophist or whether other sophists charged differently.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0099
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 235d-235e

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt

  text_sha256: 045d443d4bcff2288acc1148453afcdf2de57dc71f921e38953cd9446360a2c2
greek_terms:
  - μιμητικῆς
  - εἴδη
  - παραδείγματος
  - συμμετρίας
  - μιμήματος
observation: The Eleatic Stranger distinguishes two kinds (eide) of mimetic art (mimetike) at 235d, naming the first as eikastike techne, defined by faithful reproduction of the model's proportions in length, breadth, depth, and appropriate colors.
textual_basis: At 235d the Stranger says he discerns two eide of mimetic art; at 235d-235e he divides out eikastike techne by specifying the criterion of reproducing the paradeigma's symmetriai in three dimensions plus color.
limits: This records one branch of a dichotomy; the second branch is named only later in the dialogue. The observation does not assess whether the division is exhaustive.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0101
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 235e-236a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 235e-236a
  start_marker: 235e
  end_marker: 236a
  start_char: 37807
  end_char: 38486
  text_sha256: bea2a7df7dc72076019ca1fc71a73a705abdd5fbf69d8013f0f62d5ee07929e3
greek_terms:
  - μεγάλων
  - πλάττουσιν
  - γράφουσιν
  - συμμετρίαν
observation: The Stranger notes at 235e that sculptors or painters of large-scale works do not reproduce true proportions, because if they did the upper parts would appear too small to viewers from below. This embeds a critique of optical distortion within the definition of eikastike.
textual_basis: At 235e the Stranger says that those who sculpt or paint large works (ton megalon ... ergon) do not render the true proportion (alethinen symmetrian) because smaller upper parts would appear deficient (smiikrotera men tou deontos phainoit' an ta ano).
limits: The observation records the artisanal analogy for mimetic distinctions without claiming that the Stranger endorses or condemns such optical adjustment.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0102
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 235b-235c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 235b-235c
  start_marker: 235b
  end_marker: 235c
  start_char: 36544
  end_char: 37411
  text_sha256: e60ec94bf342a6cbf958ba810f88723334ad0ddb8d1b4b15bd2faf6bbef0870a
greek_terms:
  - θαυματοποιῶν
  - μιμητικῆς
english_gloss: wonder-workers, imitative art
observation: "The Eleatic Stranger places the sophist in the genus (genos) of wonder-workers (235b) and immediately announces that the next step is to divide the image-making art (eidolopoiike techne, 235b-235c) further. He describes the method: descending into the image-making art, if the sophist submits, to capture him and hand over the prey; otherwise to follow him through the parts (kata mere) of the mimetic art, dividing each receiving portion until he is taken (235c)."
textual_basis: 'At 235b the Stranger declares the sophist belongs to the genus of thaumatopoioi. Then at 235b-235c he says "it is resolved to divide the eidolopoiike techne as quickly as possible" and describes a two-branch procedure: capture immediately or pursue through the parts of the mimetic art by continued division. The passage explicitly maps the next diairetic step and identifies the target genus for subdivision.'
limits: The observation records the announcement of a division procedure and placement of the sophist in a genus. It does not evaluate whether the wonder-worker classification is correct or complete.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0103
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 235b-235c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 235b-235c
  start_marker: 235b
  end_marker: 235c
  start_char: 36544
  end_char: 37411
  text_sha256: e60ec94bf342a6cbf958ba810f88723334ad0ddb8d1b4b15bd2faf6bbef0870a
greek_terms:
  - βασιλικοῦ λόγου
  - ἄγραν
english_gloss: royal account, prey/capture
observation: The Eleatic Stranger frames the method of division as a hunt, using the vocabulary of capture (syllabein, 235b), prey (agran, 235c), and the directive of a "royal account" (basilikos logos, 235c). The sophist is to be seized like quarry; if he escapes into parts of the mimetic art, the inquirers are to follow and divide until he is caught.
textual_basis: 'At 235b-235c the Stranger says: if the sophist submits, "to capture him according to the orders of the royal account and hand over the prey"; if he dives into parts of the mimetic art, "to follow him, always dividing the portion that receives him, until he is taken." The hunting language (syllabein, agran) and the notion of pursuit through division sustain the metaphor across the segment.'
limits: This records the hunting metaphor for diairetic method. It does not claim that all division in the Sophist follows this metaphor, nor that the "royal account" concept is fully defined here.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0104
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 235b-235c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 235b-235c
  start_marker: 235b
  end_marker: 235c
  start_char: 36544
  end_char: 37411
  text_sha256: e60ec94bf342a6cbf958ba810f88723334ad0ddb8d1b4b15bd2faf6bbef0870a
greek_terms:
  - μετιέναι καθ’ ἕκαστά τε καὶ ἐπὶ πάντα μέθοδον
english_gloss: method that pursues individually and through all things
observation: "The Stranger describes the diairetic method in universalizing terms: \"the method of those able to pursue both individually (kath' hekasta) and through all things (epi panta)\" (235c). He claims that neither the sophist nor any other genus could ever escape it, presenting the method as exhaustive."
textual_basis: At 235c the Stranger states that no genus will ever escape the method of those who can pursue "both individually and through all things." This characterizes the division method as having universal scope, applicable to any object of inquiry, and positions it as inescapable once applied.
limits: The observation notes a methodological claim about diairetic scope. It does not assess whether the method actually succeeds, nor does it evaluate the validity of the claim that no genus can escape.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0105
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 234c-234d

  - εἴδωλα
english_gloss: to enchant, images/apparitions
observation: The Eleatic Stranger introduces a second kind of deceptive art, parallel to the painter's art of creating visual copies, now operating through speech. It enchants the young by producing verbal images about everything, making them think true things are being said.
textual_basis: At 234c, the Stranger asks whether there is some other art operating through speeches, parallel to the visual art just discussed. This art enchants through the ears, showing spoken images about all things, producing the appearance of truth rather than truth itself. The verbal copy-art is explicitly framed as analogous to the visual image-making discussed earlier in the Sophist.
limits: The observation notes a craft analogy drawn by the Stranger at 234c-234d, not an argument about the actual scope of sophistry. The passage introduces a hypothetical craft without yet naming the sophist. The relationship between verbal image-making and the sophist is proposed but not yet demonstrated within this span.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
limits: The observation records a prosopographic claim about an unnamed audience group, not a definition of sophistry or a proof about pedagogy. The characterization of youth as distant from truth is posited by the Stranger as part of the analogy, not demonstrated within the passage.
```

```yaml
textual_basis: The Stranger proposes at 231d to reckon up while resting, then counts through six appearances of the sophist. Theaetetus simply confirms each item in the enumerated list.
```

```yaml
textual_basis: The Stranger says at 231e that the sixth was disputable, but they set it down having conceded it. The qualification distinguishes this definition from the previous five, which are presented without such hedging.
limits: This observation does not explain why the sixth definition is disputable, nor does it assess whether the concession undermines the overall inquiry.
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0112
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 236a-236b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 236a-236b
  start_marker: 236a
  end_marker: 236b
  start_char: 38129
  end_char: 38884
  text_sha256: 7ad356340a2ba7c715dad973e9e839eef410dd4748b2388307ac8ead0f3a675f
greek_terms:
  - φάντασμα
  - εἰκὸς
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The Stranger defines phantasma by opposition to eikastike: eikastike produces a true likeness (eikon) that follows the real proportions of the model, while phantastike produces an appearance (phantasma) that seems to resemble the beautiful but does not genuinely do so. The opposition turns on whether the image-maker abandons the truth and follows merely apparent proportions (tas doxousas einai kalas)."
textual_basis: "At 236a the Stranger states that craftsmen (demiourgoi) abandon the truth and produce not real proportions but those that seem beautiful. At 236b he defines phantasma explicitly in opposition to eikon: it appears to resemble but does not (phainetai men, eoike de ou). The definition of each term is constituted by the contrast with the other."
limits: This observation notes the oppositional structure of the definition. It does not claim that the opposition exhausts the kinds of image-making or that the distinction remains stable throughout the dialogue.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
textual_basis: At 236a-236b craftsmen who produce large works are said to abandon true proportions and use apparent ones for the images.
limits: The analogy supports the image-making contrast only; it does not define the sophist by itself or claim every craft works this way.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0114

  - φανταστικήν

observation: "The Visitor completes the naming of the two branches of image-making at 236c: the craft that makes phantasmata rather than eikones is called phantastike, and the two kinds of eidolopoiike are summarized as eikastike and phantastike."
textual_basis: At 236c the Visitor asks whether the craft producing a phantasma rather than an eikon should be called phantastike; Theaetetus assents. The Visitor then summarizes the two kinds of eidolopoiike as eikastike and phantastike, and Theaetetus confirms.
limits: This observation records the completion and naming of the division step at 236c. It does not restate the full criterion for the branches, which is established in the immediately preceding exchange.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: The Visitor questions whether Theaetetus assented from genuine knowledge or was merely carried along by the momentum of the argument out of habituation.
textual_basis: At 236d, after Theaetetus assents to the Visitor's description of the sophist's elusiveness, the Visitor asks whether Theaetetus assents because he recognizes the point himself or because a kind of rush (rhyme) of the argument has pulled him along by habituation into assenting quickly.
limits: This observation records a procedural check on the quality of the respondent's assent; it does not determine whether Theaetetus' earlier assents were in fact defective or whether the Visitor's question itself carries dramatic irony.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
limits: This records Theaetetus's procedural concession, not a claim about the Stranger's competence or the outcome of the inquiry.
```

```yaml
limits: This observation records the marking of a definiendum; it does not assert that a definition is provided or that the inquiry succeeds.
```

```yaml
textual_basis: At 237c, when asked where one should apply the name 'that which is not,' Theaetetus replies that the question is difficult and altogether aporon for him.
limits: This records Theaetetus's stated aporia; it does not claim the inquiry is in fact impossible or that the Stranger shares this assessment.
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0119
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 233b-233c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 233b-233c

greek_terms:
  - δοξαστικὴν
  - ἐπιστήμην
  - ἀλήθειαν
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The Eleatic Stranger proposes a definitional conclusion about the sophist at 233c: the sophist has been revealed as possessing a kind of opinionative expertise about everything, not truth."
textual_basis: At 233c the Stranger states a conclusion introduced by 'therefore' (ara) claiming the sophist has been shown to possess a kind of opinionative expertise (doxastike episteme) about everything rather than truth (aletheia). The ara marks this as an inference from the preceding assent chain at 233b-c.
limits: This is the Stranger's provisional conclusion within the ongoing diairetic hunt, not a final definition of the sophist.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0120
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 233b-233c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 233b-233c
  start_marker: 233b

  end_char: 33411
  text_sha256: bdb9e74d54cf19cfb9977d5c789b4e734f2f54c371ce3ed4fc115ff315bb5b7d
greek_terms:
  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: At 233b-c the Stranger leads Theaetetus through a rapid four-step assent chain establishing that the sophist appears wise about everything to pupils but is not truly wise, concluding that the sophist possesses opinionative expertise rather than truth.
textual_basis: "The chain runs: (1) the sophist appears knowledgeable in whatever he disputes (233c), Theaetetus assents; (2) he does this about everything, Theaetetus assents; (3) therefore he appears wise to pupils, Theaetetus assents; (4) but he is not truly wise — that was shown impossible, Theaetetus again assents."
limits: This observation records the assent-chain structure, not the validity of the reasoning or whether 'impossible' was genuinely established earlier.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0121
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 238a-238b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 238a-238b
  start_marker: 238a
  end_marker: 238b
  start_char: 41942
  end_char: 42725
  text_sha256: a16c8d9dc5504c214d1f659eef58f5f3643524cf76bc5b84f7e8dc65ca31ce8a
greek_terms:
  - ὄν
  - μὴ ὄν
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Eleatic Visitor argues that it is impossible to apply number — either plurality or unity — to what is not (to me on), because number belongs among the things that are.
textual_basis: At 238a-b the Visitor states that number (arithmos) in its entirety is posited among the things that are, then infers that one cannot attach either plurality or the unit to what is not (me on). Theaetetus assents at each step.
limits: This passage does not define what is not, nor does it address whether Parmenides' prohibition on speaking of not-being can be circumvented; it only draws a consequence about number and predication.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0122
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 238a-238b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 238a-238b
  start_marker: 238a
  end_marker: 238b
  start_char: 41942
  end_char: 42725
  text_sha256: a16c8d9dc5504c214d1f659eef58f5f3643524cf76bc5b84f7e8dc65ca31ce8a
greek_terms:
  - ἀποριῶν
  - μεγίστη
  - πρώτη
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The Eleatic Visitor warns Theaetetus not to claim a conclusion prematurely: the greatest and first of the impasses (aporiai) still lies ahead, and it concerns the very starting-point of the inquiry."
textual_basis: At 238a the Visitor says mepo meg' eipes ('don't yet speak big') and identifies this as the greatest and first of the aporiai, situated at the very origin (arche) of the inquiry into not-being.
limits: The claim only announces an aporia; it does not resolve it. The passage does not specify how many aporiai there are or whether this first one is the most important for the whole dialogue.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0123
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 238a-238b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 238a-238b
  start_marker: 238a
  end_marker: 238b
  start_char: 41942
  end_char: 42725
  text_sha256: a16c8d9dc5504c214d1f659eef58f5f3643524cf76bc5b84f7e8dc65ca31ce8a
greek_terms:
  - στόματος
  - διανοίᾳ
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The Visitor frames a forced alternative: either one can utter through the mouth or grasp in thought things that are not (ta me onta) apart from number, or the whole enterprise of speaking about not-being is blocked."
textual_basis: At 238b the Visitor asks how anyone could utter through the mouth (dia tou stomatos phthenxaito) or grasp in thought (te dianoia laboi) things that are not or what is not apart from number. The rhetorical question implies that neither speech nor thought can access not-being without number, and since number belongs to being, the alternative collapses.
limits: This forced alternative operates on the premise that number is exclusively among beings; it does not examine whether not-being might admit of number in some qualified sense.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0124
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 238c-238d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 238c-238d
  start_marker: 238c
  end_marker: 238d
  start_char: 42725
  end_char: 43558
  text_sha256: 92e03371f4fe35fdc2745140a78c035543c5dc140639cb3fde2d0b5b85fcfe83
greek_terms:
  - μὴ ὂν
  - ἀδιανόητόν
  - ἄρρητον
  - ἄφθεγκτον
  - ἄλογον
english_gloss: ""
observation: At 238c, the Stranger characterizes "that which is not" with four successive alpha-privative predicates that deny its accessibility to thought, speech, and rational account.
textual_basis: "The Stranger concludes that to me on is adianoeton (unthinkable), arreton (unspeakable), aphthegkton (unutterable), and alogon (irrational). The four predicates appear in a single climactic clause, each beginning with alpha-privative, forming a rhetorical accumulation that exhausts the modes of access to something: thought, utterance, expression, and rational account."
limits: This is an assertion following from the earlier premise that one cannot attach being to non-being; it does not independently prove that non-being has these properties. The accumulation is rhetorical rather than demonstrative.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0125
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 238c-238d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 238c-238d
  start_marker: 238c
  end_marker: 238d
  start_char: 42725
  end_char: 43558
  text_sha256: 92e03371f4fe35fdc2745140a78c035543c5dc140639cb3fde2d0b5b85fcfe83
greek_terms:
  - ἐψευσάμην
  - ἀπορίαν
  - ἐλέγχοντα
  - μὴ ὂν
  - ἐναντία
english_gloss: ""
observation: "At 238c-238d, the Stranger revises his own claim about the greatest difficulty concerning non-being, announcing an even greater aporia: the attempt to refute non-being forces the refuter into self-contradiction."
textual_basis: "The Stranger explicitly says he was wrong (epseusamen, 238c) to call the previous point the greatest aporia. The greater difficulty is that the very act of refuting non-being (ton elenchonta, 238d) puts the refuter into aporia, forcing them to say contrary things (enantia) about it. This is a meta-elenctic claim: the elenchus itself becomes the subject of an elenctic observation."
limits: The Stranger only asserts that self-contradiction occurs; Theaetetus immediately asks for clarification (pos phes; eipe eti saphesteron, 238d), so the claim is not yet demonstrated within this segment.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0126
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 238c-238d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 238c-238d
  start_marker: 238c
  end_marker: 238d
  start_char: 42725
  end_char: 43558
  text_sha256: 92e03371f4fe35fdc2745140a78c035543c5dc140639cb3fde2d0b5b85fcfe83
greek_terms:
  - σαφέστερον
  - ἐν ἐμοὶ
english_gloss: ""
observation: When Theaetetus asks for a clearer explanation at 238d, the Stranger deflects by saying there is no need to look for clarity in him, redirecting attention away from his own role as explainer.
textual_basis: Theaetetus asks "how do you mean? speak still more clearly" (pos phes; eipe eti saphesteron, 238d). The Stranger responds that there is no need to look for something clearer in him (ouden dei to saphesteron en emoi skopein, 238d), turning the request back toward what has already been said rather than offering further explanation.
limits: This deflection may be a genuine pedagogical strategy rather than irony; it does not prove the Stranger is concealing knowledge or feigning ignorance.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0127
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 236e-237a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 236e-237a

greek_terms:
  - Παρμενίδης
  - ἀπεμαρτύρατο
english_gloss: ""
observation: Parmenides is named and quoted in verse as an authority witness against the claim that not-being is, at 237a.
textual_basis: The Stranger names Parmenides and says he bore witness (apemarturato), then quotes two hexameter lines from Parmenides' poem warning against the path of saying that things which are not, are.
limits: This observation records the citation of an authority; it does not evaluate whether the Stranger endorses or challenges Parmenides' prohibition.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

  - τὸ μὴ ὂν
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Stranger states that the argument has ventured to hypothesize that not-being is, at 237a.
textual_basis: The Stranger says that the argument has dared to hypothesize (hupothesthai) that what-is-not (to me on) is, framing the inquiry into falsehood as requiring this hypothesis.
limits: Records only that a hypothesis is stated; does not assess whether it is defended, accepted, or refuted.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0129
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 236e-237a

greek_terms:
  - ἀπορίας
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Stranger characterizes the problem of false appearance and false speech as perennially full of perplexity (aporia), at 236e.
textual_basis: At 236e, the Stranger says that appearing but not being, and saying things that are not true, are all full of perplexity (aporia) always in the past and now.
limits: Reports the framing language of aporia at the outset of an inquiry, not an aporia reached through elenctic examination of a respondent.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0130
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 238e-239a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 238e-239a
  start_marker: 238e
  end_marker: 239a
  start_char: 43558
  end_char: 44297
  text_sha256: 6787e2cdc6be38cad3d2ac429f69c0ced8c40ee27c42785171297015d7893f62
greek_terms:
  - ἓν
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Eleatic Stranger demonstrates that his own speech about not-being commits performative self-contradiction. At 238e he states that he posited not-being as not partaking of one or many, yet his very utterance treats it as one thing (using singular article and singular form). At 239a he adds that attaching "being" to not-being contradicts his earlier claims, and that calling it "without account, unsayable, unutterable" speaks as if to one thing.
textual_basis: 'The Stranger explicitly calls out the contradiction at 238e: he posited not-being as partaking of neither one nor many, yet he has just spoken of it as "one" by saying the phrase for not-being (using the singular article). At 239a he states that trying to attach being to it contradicts his earlier statements, and that speaking of it as without account addresses it as one thing.'
limits: This observation records the structure of the self-refutation. It does not claim that the Stranger resolves the problem, nor that Plato endorses a particular ontology. The contradiction is local to the speech about not-being in this passage.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0131
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 238e-239a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 238e-239a
  start_marker: 238e
  end_marker: 239a
  start_char: 43558
  end_char: 44297
  text_sha256: 6787e2cdc6be38cad3d2ac429f69c0ced8c40ee27c42785171297015d7893f62
greek_terms:
  - πρόσρησιν
  - διορίζειν
  - καλεῖν
english_gloss: ""
observation: At 239a the Stranger concludes that to speak correctly about not-being, one must not mark it off as one or as many, nor call it at all — since even the act of address would address it under the form of one. This is a constraint on predication and naming that goes beyond earlier claims that not-being is unsayable or unutterable.
textual_basis: At 239a the Stranger states that one must not mark it off as one or as many, nor call it at all, and explains that even by that address it would be addressed under the form of one. This moves from describing not-being as ineffable to prescribing that one must not even attempt to name it.
limits: This observation records a stated constraint on predication about not-being. It does not assess whether this constraint is later relaxed or overturned, nor does it claim this is Plato's final position on predication.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0132
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 239d-239e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 239d-239e
  start_marker: 239d
  end_marker: 239e
  start_char: 45130
  end_char: 45843
  text_sha256: 98c9fb5e538381a625b776cc8535ce180937a1d414880c95402f8dab62af7862
greek_terms:
  - εἴδωλον
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Stranger anticipates that when the sophist is called an image-maker, he will counter by demanding a definition of image. The craft-label triggers a demand for definition of the corresponding product term.
textual_basis: At 239d, the Stranger says the sophist will seize on the term image-maker and ask what in the world they mean by image (ti pote to parapan eidolon legomen).
limits: Does not show that this definition-demand is unique to the sophist; it is a standard Socratic move. Does not show whether the definition will actually be supplied.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0133
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 239d-239e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 239d-239e
  start_marker: 239d
  end_marker: 239e
  start_char: 45130
  end_char: 45843
  text_sha256: 98c9fb5e538381a625b776cc8535ce180937a1d414880c95402f8dab62af7862
greek_terms:
  - εἴδωλα
english_gloss: ""
observation: "Theaetetus answers the question what is an image by enumerating examples: reflections in water and mirrors, painted things, molded or sculpted things, and whatever other such things there are. He offers an extensional list rather than an intensional definition."
textual_basis: At 239d-239e, Theaetetus lists images in waters and mirrors (en tois hudasi kai katoptrois eidola), painted things (ta gegrammena), molded things (ta tetupomena), and other such things.
limits: Does not show whether Plato endorses or rejects definition by example. Does not address the philosophical adequacy of extensional definitions.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0134
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 239d-239e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 239d-239e
  start_marker: 239d
  end_marker: 239e
  start_char: 45130
  end_char: 45843
  text_sha256: 98c9fb5e538381a625b776cc8535ce180937a1d414880c95402f8dab62af7862
greek_terms:
  - μύειν
  - ὄμματα
  - προσποιούμενος
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The Stranger does not evaluate Theaetetus's answer directly but instead describes how the sophist would dismantle it: the sophist will pretend not to see mirrors or molded objects and will laugh at Theaetetus for addressing him as though he can see."
textual_basis: At 239e, the Stranger says the sophist will seem to Theaetetus to be shutting his eyes or having no eyes at all (muein e pantapasin ouk echein ommata) and will laugh at his words when he speaks to him as though he sees, pretending he has no mirrors (prospoioumenos oute katoptra).
limits: This is a dramatic anticipation, not an actual refutation enacted in the text. Does not show whether the sophist's tactic is philosophically legitimate or merely eristic.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0135
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 240a-240b

greek_terms:
  - εἴδωλον
  - ἀληθινόν
  - ἐοικός
  - ὄντως
inglish_gloss: ""
observation: "Theaetetus proposes a definition of image (eidolon) at 240a: that which has been likened to the true thing and is another such thing (to pros t'alēthinon aphōmoiōmenon heteron toiouton)."
textual_basis: At 240a the Eleatic Stranger asks Theaetetus to defend the sophist by defining image in one word. Theaetetus responds with the definition cited above.
limits: This is a provisional definition offered by the respondent, not the Stranger's own account. It is immediately subjected to elenctic examination in the lines that follow.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0136
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 240a-240b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 240a-240b
  start_marker: 240a
  end_marker: 240b
  start_char: 45843
  end_char: 46688
  text_sha256: f16c8d598ad4fa68b19681b8f5907136b91323abec5887f136356450299bff91
greek_terms:
  - ἐοικός
inglish_gloss: ""
observation: At 240b the Stranger forces Theaetetus to accept that the likeness (to eoikos), if it is not truly real, is 'not really being' (ouk ontōs on), and that the image (eikōn) they speak of 'is not really' though it is 'really an image.'
textual_basis: "From 240b, the Stranger elicits Theaetetus' assent that what is not true is opposite to the true, then concludes that the likeness is 'not really being' if it is not true. Theaetetus resists with 'but it is in some way' (all' esti ge mēn pōs), leading to the paradoxical formulation at 240b: 'ouk on ara [ouk] ontōs estin ontōs hēn legomen eikona.'"
limits: This records the paradox generated by the definition of image, not a settled metaphysical doctrine. The text marks Theaetetus' discomfort (all' esti ge mēn pōs) with the conclusion.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0137
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 240a-240b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 240a-240b

  end_marker: 240b

  end_char: 46688

greek_terms:
  - ποῖον;
  - λέγε οὖν καὶ ἀμύνου
inglish_gloss: ""
observation: At 240a the Eleatic Stranger reminds Theaetetus that the sophist's defense will question him only through words (ek tōn logōn), then exhorts him to 'speak and defend yourself, giving no ground to the man' (lege oun kai amynou mēden hypochōrōn ton andra).
textual_basis: The Stranger at 240a frames the inquiry as a verbal contest in which Theaetetus must stand firm against the sophist, who will interrogate him only through discourse. This binds the respondent to a defensive posture before the definition is tested.
limits: This is a procedural framing of the respondent's role, not a claim about the content of the argument that follows.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0138
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 242a-242b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt

  text_sha256: e21ad590b020052b545746ca666983200a326414c2e19b079325a8d612c1fd4d
greek_terms:
  - πατρικῷ λόγῳ
  - ἐπισκέψασθαι
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The Eleatic Stranger frames the upcoming argument as a daring attack on the paternal logos (Parmenides' doctrine) and announces the methodological starting point: examining things that currently seem clear at 242a-b."
textual_basis: At 242a the Stranger declares they must dare to attack the paternal logos or abandon the inquiry entirely. At 242b he proposes beginning with the examination of what seems clear, identifying this as the most necessary path.
limits: This observation records the procedural framing and method announcement, not the content of the Parmenidean doctrine under attack or the subsequent examination.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0139
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 242a-242b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt

greek_terms:
  - μανικὸς
  - μεταβαλὼν
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Eleatic Stranger preemptively expresses fear that Theaetetus will think he has gone mad for suddenly reversing his position regarding the Parmenidean doctrine at 242a-b.
textual_basis: At 242a-b the Stranger says he fears that because of his prior statements Theaetetus will think him mad for suddenly changing his position up and down. He adds the hedge that they will undertake the examination for Theaetetus' sake.
limits: This records the hedging move and self-characterization, not whether the Stranger actually contradicts himself or whether the fear is genuine.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0140

  - μηδὲν πλημμελεῖν
  - θαρρῶν ἴθι
english_gloss: ""
observation: Theaetetus twice gives the Stranger explicit reassurance and permission to proceed with the risky examination of the Parmenidean logos at 242a-b.
textual_basis: At 242a Theaetetus says nothing at all will hold them back. At 242b he tells the Stranger he will not think him at fault in any way and urges him to proceed boldly.
limits: This records the procedural permission and encouragement, not the content of the subsequent examination or the merits of the Stranger's self-doubt.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0141
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 240c-240d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt

greek_terms:
  - τῷ ὄντι
english_gloss: interweaving of not-being with being
observation: |
  The Eleatic Stranger claims that the sophist has compelled them to agree, against their will, that not-being somehow is. Theaetetus confirms they see this forced concession.
textual_basis: |
  At 240c, the Stranger says the many-headed sophist has compelled them, not willingly, to agree that not-being somehow is. The passage frames the concession as something extracted by the sophist's puzzle rather than voluntarily granted.
limits: |
  The observation records the text's own characterization of a concession as forced, not whether the concession is logically valid or whether the sophist figure is fairly represented.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0142
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 240c-240d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 240c-240d
  start_marker: 240c
  end_marker: 240d
  start_char: 46688
  end_char: 47471
  text_sha256: 9e1e112661a981fc349565a2d91e44842bf9bd266e4e5beb644dc6f908460ed7
greek_terms:
  - τἀναντία τοῖς οὖσι
  - τὰ μὴ ὄντα
english_gloss: false judgment thinks contraries to what is
observation: |
  The Eleatic Stranger proposes a definition of false judgment as thinking the contraries of the things that are. Theaetetus accepts this formulation without objection at 240d.
textual_basis: |
  At 240d, the Stranger asks whether false judgment will be judging the contraries to the things that are. Theaetetus answers "thus: the contraries," confirming the definition. The Stranger then immediately draws the consequence that false judgment judges the things that are not, and Theaetetus says this follows of necessity.
limits: |
  This definition is presented as a provisional step within the larger aporia about falsehood and not-being. It is not marked as a final definition. The observation does not assess whether the definition survives later scrutiny.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

english_gloss: the many-headed sophist
observation: |
  The Eleatic Stranger refers to the sophist with the epithet many-headed at 240c, characterizing the sophist as a multi-form creature rather than a single identifiable figure.
textual_basis: |
  At 240c, the Stranger says the many-headed sophist has compelled us. The epithet continues the earlier imagery of the sophist as a beast with many forms and personifies the sophist as the agent forcing the interlocutors into a problematic concession.
limits: |
  The observation records the epithet and its dramatic function. It does not make claims about whether this is a fair characterization or what specific sophists Plato may be targeting.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0144
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 241d-241e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 241d-241e
  start_marker: 241d
  end_marker: 241e
  start_char: 49069
  end_char: 49788
  text_sha256: 94efe3a795de8d7ced89a939912a35ff73ee6f4a23dd1f54ea7902140d4996f6
greek_terms:
  - πατραλοίαν
  - Παρμενίδου
english_gloss: the term parricide for attacking Parmenides
observation: At 241d the Eleatic Stranger asks Theaetetus not to suppose he is becoming a kind of parricide by attacking Parmenides' doctrine, using the term for parricide.
textual_basis: The Stranger explicitly raises the charge of parricide against himself before proceeding to examine Parmenides' logos, framing the examination as an act of defense (amunomenois) that nonetheless must test and force (basanizein, biazesthai) the paternal teaching.
limits: This does not prove the Stranger has hidden anti-Parmenidean intent; the passage marks the irony of attacking a revered predecessor through a mock-criminal analogy.
review_status: rejected
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0145
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 241d-241e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 241d-241e
  start_marker: 241d
  end_marker: 241e
  start_char: 49069
  end_char: 49788
  text_sha256: 94efe3a795de8d7ced89a939912a35ff73ee6f4a23dd1f54ea7902140d4996f6
greek_terms:
  - τό τε μὴ ὂν ὡς ἔστι κατά τι
  - τὸ ὂν αὖ πάλιν ὡς οὐκ ἔστι πῃ
english_gloss: the non-being somehow is, and being again somehow is not
observation: "At 241d-241e the Stranger formulates the two theses that the examination requires: that non-being is in some respect (kata ti), and that being in turn is not in some way (pe). These are presented as what must be forced (biazesthai) in the argument."
textual_basis: The Stranger states that defending Parmenides' logos requires testing and forcing these two claims (to me on hos esti kata ti / to on au palin hos ouk esti pe). The paired theses structure the dialectical task ahead.
limits: This observation notes the explicit thesis structure; it does not assess whether the theses are genuinely Parmenidean or a straw man.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0146
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 241d-241e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 241d-241e
  start_marker: 241d
  end_marker: 241e
  start_char: 49069
  end_char: 49788
  text_sha256: 94efe3a795de8d7ced89a939912a35ff73ee6f4a23dd1f54ea7902140d4996f6
greek_terms:
  - λόγων ψευδῶν
  - δόξης
  - εἰδώλων
  - εἰκόνων
  - μιμημάτων
  - φαντασμάτων
english_gloss: false speech, opinion, images, likenesses, imitations, phantasms
observation: At 241e the Stranger states that without refuting or agreeing on the theses about non-being and being, no one can speak about false logoi, opinion, images, likenesses, imitations, or phantasms without being ridiculous and forced to contradict themselves. This chains the sophist's target phenomena (false logos, doxa, and image-vocabulary) to the metaphysical theses.
textual_basis: The passage enumerates a list of targets — false logoi, doxa, images, likenesses, imitations, phantasms — and also mentions the arts (technai) concerned with them. The consequence chain makes investigation of non-being/being a precondition for discussing the sophist's own domain of images and falsehoods.
limits: This identifies a structural linking of domains, not a definitional success or failure.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0147
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 242c-242d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 242c-242d

  end_char: 51468
  text_sha256: 9e0a93f0861ec4c60c910a14eca2fffb0201315942fb6a496c97d5bd8ff7aaff
greek_terms:
  - μῦθόν
  - διηγεῖσθαι
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Eleatic Stranger characterizes the accounts of Parmenides and other earlier ontologists as myth-telling (muthon ... diegeisthai), framing their claims about the number and kind of beings as narratives told to an audience treated as children.
textual_basis: At 242c-d the Stranger says each of the earlier thinkers appears to him to tell a myth (muthon tina ... diegeisthai) to us as though we were children, then enumerates the one-being, two-being, and three-being doctrines as mythic narratives.
limits: This observation records the mythic framing of prior ontological accounts; it does not claim that the Stranger rejects those accounts, only that he characterizes their mode of presentation as myth.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

observation: Theaetetus interrupts the Stranger at 242c to ask him to speak more clearly (lege saphesteron ho legeis), prompting the Stranger to elaborate his critique of earlier thinkers at length.
textual_basis: At 242c Theaetetus says lege saphesteron ho legeis, a direct request for clarification, after the Stranger warns against hasty agreement. The Stranger then launches into the extended myth-analysis that fills the rest of the segment.
limits: This records a procedural turn-taking move; it does not interpret Theaetetus's motivation or assess whether the clarification succeeds.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0149
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 242c-242d
source_ref:

greek_terms:
  - παισὶν
  - ὡς οὖσιν ἡμῖν
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Eleatic Stranger claims that each earlier ontologist appears to tell a myth to the present interlocutors as though they were children (paisin hos ousin hemin), framing his own audience as the infantile recipients of earlier teaching.
textual_basis: At 242c the Stranger says each earlier thinker appears to narrate a myth to us as though we are children (paisin hos ousin hemin). The simile characterizes the interlocutors (and by extension the Stranger himself) as recipients of childish stories, not as philosophical peers addressed by rigorous argument.
limits: This observation marks the infantalizing characterization; it does not claim that the Stranger intends irony or that the earlier thinkers actually addressed children.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0150
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 243b-243c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 243b-243c
  start_marker: 243b
  end_marker: 243c
  start_char: 52115
  end_char: 52962
  text_sha256: 916b5df7e0cc197c6bc305a19538b8d9970784227d904b635ae731b69761b186
greek_terms:
  - τὸ μὴ ὄν
  - ἀπορίας
english_gloss: the not-being; perplexity
observation: |
  The Eleatic Stranger confesses that when he was younger he thought he understood "not-being" precisely, but now he finds himself in aporia about it. He contrasts his former confidence with his present perplexity (243b).
textual_basis: |
  At 243b the Stranger says explicitly that when he was younger he believed he understood precisely (akribos) whenever someone mentioned "not-being," but now (nyn) he sees the depth of the perplexity (aporia) about it. The confession is autobiographical and marks a shift from claimed knowledge to acknowledged ignorance.
limits: |
  The observation records a local aporia confession; it does not establish whether this aporia is sincere or ironic, nor does it assess whether the Stranger's former confidence was warranted.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0151
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 243b-243c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 243b-243c
  start_marker: 243b
  end_marker: 243c
  start_char: 52115
  end_char: 52962
  text_sha256: 916b5df7e0cc197c6bc305a19538b8d9970784227d904b635ae731b69761b186
greek_terms:
  - θάτερον
english_gloss: being; the other
observation: |
  The Stranger claims that people suffer from the same perplexity about "being" as they do about "not-being," but they only admit it in the latter case. He asserts that they are in a similar state (homoiōs echontes) toward both (243c).
textual_basis: |
  At 243c the Stranger says that just as they are in aporia about not-being, perhaps they have the same condition (tauton touto pathos) in the soul concerning being: they claim to be well-supplied (euporein) and to understand when someone utters "being," but about the other (thateron, i.e., not-being) they do not — yet they are similarly disposed toward both. This constitutes a diagnosis of asymmetrical self-awareness about symmetrical ignorance.
limits: |
  This observation records the Stranger's diagnostic claim about others' cognitive state. It does not assess whether the claim about symmetrical ignorance is correct, nor does it address whether the Stranger includes himself in this diagnosis.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

  []
english_gloss: the many; we will consider
observation: |
  The Stranger announces that consideration of "the many" will be postponed until after the current discussion (243c). This closes the present line of inquiry and sets an agenda for later.
textual_basis: |
  At 243c the Stranger says: "About the many then, we will consider [skeptomai] after this" (peri men toinyn tōn pollōn kai meta touto skepsometh'). The phrase marks an explicit procedural boundary and a promise to return to a deferred topic.
limits: |
  The observation records a procedural deferral. It does not establish what "the many" refers to or whether the promised return occurs later in the dialogue.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0153

greek_terms:
  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Eleatic Stranger, after admitting his own aporia about the meaning of being, turns the role of answerer over to the partisans of dualism and monism. He asks them to make clear what they mean when they say 'being' and proposes to question them, but Theaetetus stands in as the respondent who will speak for both camps.
textual_basis: "At 244a the Stranger says 'since we have run into aporia, you make clear to us what you intend to signify when you utter 'being'' and proceeds to address first the pluralists, then at 244b the monists, with Theaetetus answering on their behalf (244a: 'didaskete oun proton tout' auto hemas...', 244b: 'tode toinyn apokrinesthon')."
limits: This observation records the procedural transfer of the interrogator's role but does not assess the philosophical adequacy of the Eleatic Stranger's strategy or whether the ventriloquism through Theaetetus is fair to the absent interlocutors.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: Theaetetus is cast as the respondent who will answer for absent ontological camps. The Eleatic Stranger addresses him directly and Theaetetus supplies short affirmative responses confirming the Stranger's formulations of monist doctrine and even completing the Stranger's imagined quotation of the monists' own speech.
textual_basis: "Throughout 244a-244b Theaetetus answers the Stranger with short affirmations: 'orthotata eirekas' (244a), 'hekista ge' (244a), and 'nai' (244b) three times in rapid succession. At 244b he is asked 'on kaleite ti?' and replies 'nai', illustrating the density and brevity of respondent turn-taking in this passage."
limits: This observation records the form of Theaetetus's responses as a turn-taking pattern; it does not assess whether his short assents constitute genuine agreement or merely procedural compliance.
```

```yaml
  - τὸ ὂν
  - τοῦ μεγίστου τε καὶ ἀρχηγοῦ
english_gloss: The greatest and the first principle — being itself.
observation: |
  The Eleatic Stranger announces that after the present point in the discussion, they must investigate "the greatest and the first leader" first — and Theaetetus immediately identifies this as a question about what those who speak of "being" think they make clear by the term.
textual_basis: |
  At 243d the Stranger says they must now examine the greatest and ruling principle first. Theaetetus responds by making the referent explicit: he asks whether the Stranger means they must first investigate being, and what those who speak of it think they indicate by it. The passage frames the inquiry into being as the primary task before examining anything else.
limits: |
  This records the dramatic programmatic statement at 243d and Theaetetus's identification of the target. It does not analyze the subsequent argument about the pluralists or the dialectical cross-examination that follows.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

greek_terms:
  - οἷον αὐτῶν παρόντων ἀναπυνθανομένους
english_gloss: As if questioning them while they were present.
observation: |
  The Stranger proposes an investigative method for examining the earlier thinkers' claims about being: they will proceed as though questioning those absent thinkers while they were present (243d). This creates an embedded dramatic frame where past philosophers are treated as virtual interlocutors.
textual_basis: |
  At 243d the Stranger says they must conduct the inquiry as if questioning them while they were present. The subsequent speech in 243d-e then directly addresses pluralist thinkers in the second person, enacting the cross-examination of absent parties.
limits: |
  This notes the explicit framing device. It does not evaluate whether the absent thinkers are fairly represented or whether the device commits a fallacy.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

  - γένεσιν
  - οὐσίας
  - εἴδεσιν
  - σῶμα
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The Eleatic Stranger characterizes two opposed ontological parties: those who place being in forms (en eidesi) and those who drag everything into body by force."
textual_basis: At 246c the Stranger describes one group as "those who tear things into small pieces in their accounts" and call it a carried genesis rather than ousia. At 246c-d he contrasts them with those who place being in forms (hēmerōteroi, gentler), and then at 246d describes the body-draggers as violent (biāi) and difficult or nearly impossible to engage.
limits: This observation records the dramatic characterization of doctrinal parties; it does not assess whether the characterization is fair or accurate.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: The Stranger frames the inquiry by describing a vast battle (apletos machē) between two parties over ousia and then proposes to take an account (logon) from each party in turn.
textual_basis: At 246c the Stranger says "an immense battle between both sides always stands concerning these things, Theaetetus" and then at 246c-d announces "let us take an account from both kinds, part by part, concerning the ousia they posit." Theaetetus asks how, and the Stranger lays out a differentiated procedure.
limits: This observation records a procedural framing move; it does not evaluate the truth of either party's position.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0160
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 246c-246d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 246c-246d
  start_marker: 246c
  end_marker: 246d
  start_char: 58800
  end_char: 59603
  text_sha256: 28bc80ab5955672551a5d57f8ae0839b44f26b15b2c45d06108f78c9534b2cf6
greek_terms:
  - βελτίους
  - λόγῳ
  - ἔργῳ
  - τἀληθὲς ζητοῦμεν
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Stranger proposes that if the bodily party cannot be improved in deed (ergōi), they should be made better in speech (logōi) by supposing them willing to answer more lawfully; he then subordinates the distinction between better and worse respondents to the goal of seeking truth (talēthes zētoumen).
textual_basis: At 246d the Stranger says "if it were possible in deed to make them better, but if this is not feasible, let us do it in speech, supposing they would be willing to answer more lawfully than now." He adds that agreements from better people are presumably more authoritative than from worse, but then insists "we do not care about these things, but we seek the truth."
limits: This observation records a methodological priority statement; it does not assess whether the procedure succeeds or whether the bodily party's views are accurately represented.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0164
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 249b-249c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 249b-249c
  start_marker: 249b
  end_marker: 249c
  start_char: 64453
  end_char: 65269
  text_sha256: 47d9c8d64372b8333550add5f93700df5eab58871e38b5554bf47b4f5f8153c9
greek_terms:
  - ἀκινήτων
  - νοῦν
  - κινούμενα
english_gloss: From things at rest / motionless things
observation: The Eleatic Stranger shows that both the hypothesis that only unchanging things are (the 'Friends of Forms' position, though not named here) and the hypothesis that everything is in motion each eliminate the possibility of nous, and that both eliminative consequences are unacceptable.
textual_basis: At 249b-c, the Stranger first states that if only unchanging things are, nous cannot belong to anyone about anything anywhere. He then states that if everything is in motion, the same result follows — nous is also eliminated from the things that are. Both consequences are treated as equally unacceptable, forcing the philosopher to resist both positions.
limits: The Stranger does not name the 'Friends of Forms' explicitly here; that identification comes earlier. The passage does not resolve the dilemma — it only flags that both extremes produce an eliminative contradiction regarding nous.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0165
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 249b-249c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 249b-249c
  start_marker: 249b

  end_char: 65269
  text_sha256: 47d9c8d64372b8333550add5f93700df5eab58871e38b5554bf47b4f5f8153c9
greek_terms:
  - μαχετέον
  - παντὶ λόγῳ
english_gloss: must be fought / with every argument
observation: "The Stranger issues an explicit combat mandate: one must fight with every argument against anyone who eliminates episteme, phronesis, or nous from the things that are."
textual_basis: At 249c the Stranger declares that against this man one must fight with every argument. The target is anyone who, in eliminating knowledge or thought or intellect, insists on something in any way whatsoever. This frames the inquiry as a polemical, not merely dialectical, enterprise.
limits: The combat mandate is directed at a specific type of opponent (one who eliminates cognitive capacities), not at all discourse. The passage does not specify what kind of fighting this is beyond argumentative resistance.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0166

greek_terms:
  - ἐπιστήμην
  - φρόνησιν
  - νοῦν
  - ἀφανίζων
english_gloss: knowledge, thought, intellect — eliminating
observation: The Stranger groups episteme, phronesis, and nous as a triad of cognitive capacities whose elimination from the things that are is the unacceptable consequence that both extreme ontological positions share.
textual_basis: At 249c, the Stranger lists three terms together — episteme or phronesis or nous — and says one must fight against anyone who eliminates them. The earlier argument at 249b-c already showed that both the 'all is at rest' and 'all is in motion' positions eliminate nous; now the Stranger broadens the threatened cognitive set to include episteme and phronesis alongside nous.
limits: The passage does not define the differences among episteme, phronesis, and nous. The triad is presented as equally threatened, not as a hierarchy. Whether this triad is a Platonic innovation or a report of earlier views is not determined here.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: The Stranger frames the inclusion of both immobile and moved things under being as like a children's prayer.
textual_basis: At 249d-249e the figure introduces the both-together proposal immediately before the Stranger says the inquiry remains in deep ignorance.
limits: This records the local childish-wish framing, not old-man self-deprecation or a settled ontological solution.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0172
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 249d-249e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt

  start_marker: 249d
  end_marker: 249e
  start_char: 65269
  end_char: 65974
  text_sha256: 1153304071e18f2e595c092e75c7bf790d82b81b89a3f80470e6e6b8c705f1e8
greek_terms:
  - συναμφότερα
english_gloss: both together
observation: "The Stranger proposes a middle position between two opposed ontological camps: those who say all being is at rest and those who say all being is in motion. His solution is synamphotera — that being and the whole must be said to include both rest and motion (249d)."
textual_basis: At 249d the Stranger characterizes the two opposed positions (eide legonton to pan hestekos versus to on kinounton) and refuses both extremes, instead proposing to include all immobile and moved things synamphotera — both together — under being and the whole. This is a dialectical move that synthesizes opposed positions by a dichotomous framing.
limits: This observation notes the structural move of combining opposed positions; it does not evaluate whether the Stranger's synamphotera proposal resolves the underlying aporia. The Stranger immediately (249d-e) acknowledges that this apparent solution has not yet been examined.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0173
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 249d-249e

  end_char: 65974
  text_sha256: 1153304071e18f2e595c092e75c7bf790d82b81b89a3f80470e6e6b8c705f1e8
greek_terms:
  - ἐν ἀγνοίᾳ τῇ πλείστῃ
  - φαινόμεθα δέ τι λέγειν ἡμῖν αὐτοῖς
english_gloss: in the greatest ignorance ... we appear to ourselves to be saying something
observation: The Stranger immediately undercuts the apparent solution he has just proposed, telling Theaetetus that they are now in the greatest ignorance about being, and that they only appear to themselves to be saying something (249d-e).
textual_basis: At 249d-e the Stranger exclaims that they now seem to be about to recognize the aporia of the investigation (249d), then at 249e states 'we are now in the greatest ignorance about it, while we appear to ourselves to be saying something' (en agnoiai te pleistei peri autou, phainometha de ti legein hemin autois). This is a knowledge disavowal that retroactively casts doubt on the synamphotera proposal just made.
limits: This observation records the knowledge disavowal pattern; it does not assess whether the Stranger's self-correction is sincere or a dialectical strategy to prepare Theaetetus for further inquiry.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0174
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 248e-249a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 248e-249a
  start_marker: 248e
  end_marker: 249a
  start_char: 63635
  end_char: 64453
  text_sha256: 2b5d339bcfc563270409e7e6063af14236091335c0b0412ef2eecc762831ad41
greek_terms:
  - κίνησιν
  - ζωὴν
  - ψυχὴν
  - φρόνησιν
  - σεμνὸν καὶ ἅγιον
  - νοῦν οὐκ ἔχον
observation: |
  The Eleatic Stranger presents Theaetetus with a forced rhetorical alternative regarding the nature of that which completely is (to pantelos on). Either it possesses motion, life, soul, and thought, or it is solemn and holy, lacking intellect, motionless, and standing still. Theaetetus responds that conceding the second option would be a terrible concession (deinon logon), which pressures the respondent toward accepting the first horn.
textual_basis: |
  At 248e-249a the Stranger asks whether they will be easily persuaded that motion and life and soul and thought are absent from that which completely is, and that it neither lives nor thinks but is solemn and holy, without intellect, motionless, standing still (249a). Theaetetus replies "terrible indeed, Stranger, would be the account we would concede" (deinon mentan, o xene, logon synchoroumen), treating the two options as exhaustive.
limits: |
  The observation registers the structure of the interrogative turn as a forced binary. It does not assess whether the Stranger endorses either horn, nor does it claim that the alternative is logically exhaustive. It also does not evaluate the metaphysical position itself.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0175
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 248e-249a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 248e-249a
  start_marker: 248e
  end_marker: 249a
  start_char: 63635
  end_char: 64453
  text_sha256: 2b5d339bcfc563270409e7e6063af14236091335c0b0412ef2eecc762831ad41
greek_terms:
  - ποιεῖν
  - πάσχειν
  - γιγνωσκομένην
  - γνώσεως
  - κινεῖσθαι
  - ἠρεμοῦν
observation: |
  At 248e the Stranger advances an argument that builds on the previously established premise that knowing acts (poiein) and the known undergoes (paschein). From this premise he infers that being (ousia), insofar as it is known by knowledge, is moved through being affected — something that, as agreed, could not happen to that which is at rest. This inference then generates the dramatic forced alternative about whether being possesses life and motion.
textual_basis: |
  The Stranger states at 248e that according to this account (kata ton logon touton), being, insofar as it is known by knowledge, is moved because of being affected (kineisthai dia to paschein), which they have said could not occur concerning that which is at rest. Theaetetus assents with "correct" (orthos), confirming the prior agreement. The Stranger then immediately leverages this result at 248e-249a to pose the question whether motion and life are absent from that which completely is.
limits: |
  The observation notes the structural dependence of the 248e-249a argument on a prior premise about acting and undergoing. It does not evaluate whether the acting/undergoing premise was correctly established earlier in the dialogue, nor does it assess the soundness of the inference drawn from it.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0176

greek_terms:
  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Eleatic Stranger re-enacts an earlier dialectical procedure by turning Theaetetus into the respondent, explicitly framing the questioning as a repetition of what they had earlier asked others about a hot-and-cold universe.
textual_basis: At 250a the Stranger says they would justly be asked what they themselves once asked those who say the all is hot and cold, and announces he will question Theaetetus just as he did those earlier interlocutors then, so that the conversation may advance.
limits: This observation registers the explicit procedural frame. It does not assess whether the re-enactment is pedagogically distinct from the original inquiry or whether Theaetetus's responses differ in substance from those of the earlier interlocutors.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0177
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 250a-250b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 250a-250b
  start_marker: 250a
  end_marker: 250b
  start_char: 65974
  end_char: 66749
  text_sha256: 5afb8b82cab976a9f39a96572e60021507a34bd4b5111428fcf3339e1b3cc97d
greek_terms:
  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The Stranger presses Theaetetus through a forced choice: if motion and rest both are, does being entail being moved or being at rest? When Theaetetus denies both options, the Stranger infers a third thing beyond motion and rest, namely being, in which motion and rest are encompassed."
textual_basis: At 250a-b the Stranger asks whether saying both motion and rest are means they are moved (denied at 250b) or at rest (denied at 250b). The Stranger then posits being as a third thing beside motion and rest that encompasses them by their sharing in ousia (250b).
limits: This observation tracks the elenctic structure of the forced alternative and its resolution into a third term. It does not adjudicate whether the argument is sound or whether the third term is ultimately distinct from motion and rest in Parmenidean terms.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0178

greek_terms:
  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Stranger introduces being as a third thing beyond the pair of motion and rest, posited in the soul and serving as that in which motion and rest are encompassed through their communion with ousia.
textual_basis: At 250b the Stranger says to ti theion — positing being as some third thing beside these in the soul — and describes motion and rest as encompassed by being through their sharing (koinonia) with ousia.
limits: This observation records the introduction of being as a third entity distinct from motion and rest and the use of communion language. It does not evaluate whether this anticipates the later discussion of the communion of kinds or whether being here is a form in the technical sense.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0179
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 247d-247e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 247d-247e
  start_marker: 247d
  end_marker: 247e
  start_char: 61124
  end_char: 61954
  text_sha256: 0bd90ffcd383fc9169cc4b89e54ae1dbc5dcfb2556f1cf4472ef579af6bb5ecd
greek_terms:
  - δύναμις
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The Eleatic Stranger offers a provisional definition (horos) of being: whatever possesses any power (dunamis) either to act upon or to be affected by anything else, even once and to the smallest degree, truly is. He frames it explicitly by saying 'I set down a boundary' (horon tithemai) for beings as nothing other than power."
textual_basis: "At 247d the Stranger signals a difficulty (aporoien) and proposes a test: whether the materialists would accept a certain kind of being. At 247e he delivers the definition, marked by tithēmi gar horon ('I set down a definition') and the formula to on ... ouk allo ti plēn dunamis ('being is nothing other than power'). Theatetus accepts on behalf of the absent materialists."
limits: The definition is explicitly provisional and hypothetical — it is offered on behalf of absent interlocutors (the materialists), not as the Stranger's own settled doctrine. The passage does not establish whether this definition will survive later scrutiny.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0180
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 247d-247e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 247d-247e
  start_marker: 247d
  end_marker: 247e
  start_char: 61124
  end_char: 61954
  text_sha256: 0bd90ffcd383fc9169cc4b89e54ae1dbc5dcfb2556f1cf4472ef579af6bb5ecd
greek_terms:
  - συμφυὲς γεγονός
  - ἀσώματον
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Stranger identifies the target of the definition by surveying the position of rival ontologists. He notes that materialists (those who define being as body) concede only grudgingly that something incorporeal (asōmaton) exists, and that the definition must cover both bodily and incorporeal things as a unified kind (symphues gegonos) that both camps can look to and call being.
textual_basis: At 247d the Stranger describes how the materialists 'concede grudgingly a small amount of incorporeal being' (smikron ethelousi tōn ontōn synchōrein asōmaton), then states that what is 'grown together' (symphues gegonos) from both bodily and incorporeal things must be what the definition states. This prefaces the dunamis definition at 247e by framing it as needing to satisfy both camps.
limits: This observation records the dialectical framing of the definition. It does not assess whether the Stranger's survey of positions is accurate or whether the definition succeeds in unifying both camps.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0181
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 247d-247e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 247d-247e
  start_marker: 247d
  end_marker: 247e
  start_char: 61124
  end_char: 61954
  text_sha256: 0bd90ffcd383fc9169cc4b89e54ae1dbc5dcfb2556f1cf4472ef579af6bb5ecd
greek_terms:
  - ποιεῖν
english_gloss: ""
observation: The dunamis definition is structured around the pair of acting (poiein) and being affected (paschein). Being is defined as whatever is fitted by nature either to act upon another thing (poiein heteron hotioun) or to be affected (pathein) even in the slightest way by the most trivial thing, even if only once. This patient-agent axis serves as the criterion for inclusion among beings.
textual_basis: "At 247e the Stranger defines being in terms of dunamis eit' eis to poiein heteron hotioun pephukos eit' eis to pathein ('a power either to act upon another thing whatsoever or to be affected'). The disjunctive structure makes acting and being affected the two alternative modes of the relevant power. The Stranger emphasizes the minimal threshold: 'even the slightest [being affected] by the most trivial thing, even if only once.'"
limits: This observation notes the disjunctive agent-patient structure of the definition. It does not claim that all beings must both act and be affected; the definition only requires one or the other. It does not address whether the later dialogue will treat this pair as exhaustive.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0182

  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Eleatic Stranger frames the inquiry about the communion of being, motion, and rest as a trilemma, offering three alternative theses for Theaetetus to choose among.
textual_basis: At 251d the Stranger asks whether we should posit that (1) nothing participates in anything else (all are unmixed), (2) everything participates with everything, or (3) some things do and some do not (ta men, ta de me). The question is presented as a single choice among three exhaustive logical possibilities.
limits: This observation records the trilemma structure as a procedural turn; it does not assess which option is correct or trace the subsequent argument.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
limits: This records an aporia moment; it does not assess whether Theaetetus's inability is genuine or staged.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

observation: "When Theaetetus cannot answer, the Stranger proposes a new procedure: take each hypothesis one at a time and examine the consequences (ta symbainonta) rather than choosing among them at once."
textual_basis: At 251e the Stranger asks why Theaetetus does not take them one by one (kath' hen apokrinomenos eph' hekastou ta symbainonta eskepso). Theaetetus assents to this procedural shift.
limits: This records a procedural intervention; it does not evaluate whether the method is effective or whether Theaetetus should have been able to answer directly.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0186
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 250e-251a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 250e-251a
  start_marker: 250e
  end_marker: 251a
  start_char: 67559
  end_char: 68356
  text_sha256: 1fe732c17f5a6f9cf1693b37e26845ad36d912ce861b4ecf31cc408df9390e14
greek_terms:
  - ἐξ ἴσου τό τε ὂν καὶ τὸ μὴ ὂν ἀπορίας μετειλήφατον
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Stranger explicitly brackets the preceding aporia about being and not-being before launching a new inquiry into predication. He states that being and not-being have shared equally in the difficulty and that this difficulty will be set aside, with the hope that clarity about one may illuminate the other (250e-251a).
textual_basis: At 250e, the Stranger says 'let this be set aside here as having been thoroughly puzzled through' (entoûtha keísthō diēporēménon), explicitly pausing the being/not-being investigation. He then frames the hope that if one of the two becomes clearer, the other will appear likewise, before turning to the many-names problem at 251a.
limits: This observation records the structural bracketing move. It does not claim that the aporia is resolved or that being and not-being are genuinely parallel; the Stranger only asserts they share equally in difficulty at this juncture.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0187
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 250e-251a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 250e-251a

  end_char: 68356
  text_sha256: 1fe732c17f5a6f9cf1693b37e26845ad36d912ce861b4ecf31cc408df9390e14
greek_terms:
  - ἄνθρωπον
  - χρώματα
  - σχήματα
  - μεγέθη
  - κακίας
  - ἀρετάς
english_gloss: ""
observation: "Theaetetus asks for an example (paradeigma), and the Stranger responds with an example rather than a definition or abstract principle. He illustrates the problem of one thing called by many names by enumerating attributes predicated of a human being: colors, shapes, sizes, vices, and virtues, plus countless other things (250e-251a)."
textual_basis: At 250e-251a, Theaetetus asks 'what sort of thing? give an example' and the Stranger provides the human-being example. The list mixes physical properties (colors, shapes, sizes) with moral attributes (vices, virtues), and extends to countless others beyond those enumerated.
limits: This records the use of paradeigma as a method in response to a request. It does not evaluate whether the example is well-chosen or whether it ultimately helps resolve the many-names problem. The passage breaks off mid-sentence at 251a with 'not only', so the full scope of the example is not yet given.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0189
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 250c-250d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 250c-250d
  start_marker: 250c
  end_marker: 250d
  start_char: 66749
  end_char: 67559
  text_sha256: 7ef61cd825ad65ffb58396aafe8f248020bd79f32e402c98f230706c6761e401
greek_terms:
  - κίνησις
  - στάσις
  - τὸ ὄν
english_gloss: The Eleatic Visitor infers that being (to on) is a third thing beyond motion and rest, since it is predicated of both.
observation: At Sophist 250c, the Eleatic Visitor concludes that being (to on) is something other than both motion and rest together — a third thing — because being is predicated of both while neither motion alone nor rest alone is identical with being. At 250d he restates this as being 'revealed outside (ektos) of both' motion and rest.
textual_basis: "The Visitor says at 250c: 'Then according to its own nature being neither rests nor moves' (kata ten hautou physin ara to on oute hesteken oute kineitai). When Theaetetus agrees, the Visitor draws the consequence that being is heteron de ti touton (something other than these), i.e., a third thing beyond the pair of opposites. At 250d he explicitly says to on […] ektos touton amphoteron anapephantai (being has been revealed outside both of them)."
limits: This observation records the inference within this segment only. It does not track whether this 'third thing' status creates a new aporia about being's own nature or how the argument resolves it later. It also does not assess whether the underlying assumption (that a common predicate shared by two items must name a third distinct item) is valid.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0190
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 250c-250d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 250c-250d
  start_marker: 250c
  end_marker: 250d
  start_char: 66749
  end_char: 67559
  text_sha256: 7ef61cd825ad65ffb58396aafe8f248020bd79f32e402c98f230706c6761e401
greek_terms:
  - τρίτον
english_gloss: Theaetetus uses the verb apomanteuesthai (to divine/prophesy) and the Visitor uses apopeirasthe (to attempt/probe) to characterize their inquiry about being.
observation: At 250c, Theaetetus characterizes their conclusion about being as a third thing with the verb apomanteuesthai (we are divining), a tentative epistemological marker. The Visitor at 250c-d reinforces the difficulty by saying it is 'in no way easy' (oudamose eti rhadion) to know where to turn thought, and at 250d he recalls their earlier aporia about not-being, saying 'we were held by every perplexity' (pase syneschometha aporia).
textual_basis: "Theaetetus at 250c: 'we are in danger of truly divining some third thing (triton apomanteuesthai ti) — being.' The Visitor at 250c: 'where must one yet turn thought? … in no way easy.' At 250d the Visitor recalls the prior aporia about not-being: 'concerning the name of not-being … we were held by every perplexity (pase syneschometha aporia).' The visitor connects the current aporia about being to the earlier aporia about not-being, framing both as parallel difficulties."
limits: This observation records the epistemological tentativeness markers and the recall of the prior aporia. It does not claim that the dialogue remains aporetic or that this aporia is unresolved; the Visitor may subsequently resolve it.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0191
source_work: Sophist
```
