```yaml
  start_char: 71698
  end_char: 72453
  text_sha256: 25eadfb56b317ada9ebd2d455025c249fa2e49555559769bef3d5865cef64afd
greek_terms:
  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The Eleatic Stranger presents Theaetetus with a structured choice among exactly three alternatives regarding the mixing of kinds: all mix, none mix, or some mix and some do not. He eliminates two alternatives as already disproved and forces Theaetetus to affirm the remaining one."
textual_basis: At 252e the Stranger states that one of three things is necessary — either all, or none, or some things are willing to mix and others not. He then declares that the first two alternatives have already been found impossible and concludes that anyone wishing to answer correctly must posit the remaining one of the three. This is a formal elimination structure reducing a respondent to a single answer.
limits: This observation records the formal structure of the choice and elimination; it does not evaluate whether the prior refutations of the two alternatives are sound. It does not address the metaphysical content of what does or does not mix.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0192
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 252e-253a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 252e-253a
  start_marker: 252e
  end_marker: 253a
  start_char: 71698
  end_char: 72453
  text_sha256: 25eadfb56b317ada9ebd2d455025c249fa2e49555559769bef3d5865cef64afd
greek_terms:
  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The Stranger compares the mixing of kinds to letters (grammata): some letters do not fit together, while others do, and vowels in particular run through all the others like a bond, such that without a vowel it is impossible to fit one letter to another. He then asks whether everyone knows which letters can combine with which, or whether a craft (techne) is needed, and Theaetetus answers that a craft is required — naming it grammar (grammatike)."
textual_basis: "At 252e-253a the Stranger says that when some things mix and others do not, they are like letters: some letters do not harmonize with each other, while others do. He adds that vowels differ from the others in running through all like a bond, so that without a vowel it is impossible to fit any other letter to another. He then asks whether everyone knows which letters can combine with which or whether a craft is needed, and Theaetetus identifies the craft as grammar."
limits: This observation records the analogy between kinds-mixing and letter-combination, including the special role of vowels and the claim that a craft governs this knowledge. It does not claim that the analogy is perfectly exact or that grammar is the Stranger's final account of dialectic.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

greek_terms:
  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Stranger constructs a three-option disjunction, eliminates two options by appeal to a prior finding, and secures Theaetetus's assent to the remaining option without further argument. Theaetetus's role is reduced to confirming the elimination and accepting the survivor.
textual_basis: At 252e the Stranger lays out the triad of all, none, or some mix. He then declares the first two impossible, and Theaetetus assents. The Stranger concludes that the remaining one must be posited, and Theaetetus agrees fully. Theaetetus contributes no independent reasoning.
limits: This observation records the structural elimination procedure; it does not assess whether the prior finding was correctly established, nor whether the third alternative is genuinely the only remaining possibility. It does not claim that the elimination is sophistical.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
  Theaetetus deploys a reductio against unrestricted communion of all things: if motion and rest could participate in each other, motion itself would completely stop and rest itself would move, which is impossible by the greatest necessities.
textual_basis: |
  At 252d Theaetetus says that if motion and rest were to combine with each other (epigignoisthēn ep'allēloin), motion itself would entirely stand still and rest itself would in turn move. The Visitor confirms this is impossible by the greatest necessities (tais megistais anankais).
limits: |
  The observation records the reductio structure but does not assess whether this exhausts the possibilities for the communion of kinds.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
textual_basis: |
  At 252d the Visitor says to triton dē monon loipon (the third alone remains), and Theaetetus answers nai. This follows the rejection of total separation (252c) and total communion (252d), framing the inquiry as a forced choice among three alternatives with two eliminated.
limits: |
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0198
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 251b-251c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 251b-251c
  start_marker: 251b
  end_marker: 251c
  start_char: 68356
  end_char: 69139
  text_sha256: 60a080433ddc73412c0cfb10981e2ccb9cb4730c5241af83a46e385048d80663
greek_terms:
  - θοίνην παρεσκευάκαμεν
  - ὀψιμαθέσι
  - πρόχειρον
english_gloss: We have prepared a feast for the young and for late-learners among the old.
observation: "The Eleatic Stranger characterizes the 'one-many' puzzle as a feast he has prepared, and describes those who seize on it—both young and elderly late-learners—as delighting in forbidding speech like 'good man.' The tone is gently satirical: the puzzle is a banquet prepared for the intellectually immature."
textual_basis: At 251b the Stranger says he has prepared a feast for the young and for the late-learners among the old. He adds that it is ready-to-hand for anyone to object that many cannot be one, and that they rejoice in forbidding expressions like 'good man' at 251c. The feast metaphor frames the common sophistical objection as a treat for the unseasoned, which is ironic given the seriousness of the Eleatic challenge.
limits: Does not prove the Stranger considers the one-many problem itself trivial; the irony targets the superficial use of the puzzle by those who lack philosophical depth. The feast image may also serve a pedagogical function of easing the entry into a difficult topic.
review_status: rejected
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0201
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 251b-251c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 251b-251c
  start_marker: 251b
  end_marker: 251c
  start_char: 68356
  end_char: 69139
  text_sha256: 60a080433ddc73412c0cfb10981e2ccb9cb4730c5241af83a46e385048d80663
greek_terms:
  - νέοις
  - ὀψιμαθέσι
  - εὐθὺς
  - ἀντιλαβέσθαι
  - πρόχειρον
  - χαίρουσιν
  - οὐκ ἐῶντες
observation: The Stranger reports that some interlocutors, as soon as the one-many problem is raised, immediately seize on it as something ready-to-hand and take pleasure in forbidding the ordinary predication that uses mixed terms. This describes a rhetorical move that blocks dialectical inquiry before it can develop, by turning a genuine metaphysical problem into a simplistic prohibition.
textual_basis: At 251b the Stranger says that immediately anyone can seize upon it, that it is impossible for the many to be one and the one many, and adds that they rejoice in forbidding expressions such as 'good man.' The gesture of immediate seizure is a procedural move that blocks inquiry rather than advancing it.
limits: The observation identifies a pattern of elenctic obstruction; it does not assess whether the one-many problem is ultimately soluble. The Stranger's tone suggests the objection is premature, but he does not yet refute it.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0202

greek_terms:
  - πότερα
  - γένη
  - ταὐτόν
  - θάτερον
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The Eleatic Stranger presents Theaetetus with a forced alternative about the status of Same and Other: either they are two additional genera beyond the three already discussed (Being, Rest, Motion), making the investigation concern five kinds; or they are being called by names that actually belong to one of the existing three kinds without the interlocutors realizing it."
textual_basis: "At 254e the Stranger uses a disjunctive question structure (potera...e) to present exactly two options: either Same and Other are two additional genera beyond the three, or the speakers have unknowingly been calling one of the existing three by the names Same and Other. Theaetetus responds 'perhaps' without choosing either branch."
limits: This observation records the structure of the alternative. It does not claim that the alternative is exhaustive or that the investigation ultimately adopts one branch rather than the other.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0203
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 254e-255a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 254e-255a
  start_marker: 254e
  end_marker: 255a
  start_char: 75954
  end_char: 76640
  text_sha256: cebbe0afa9913c91d5feb9c0f2dfc709e50f31b655555e2804d73fdf02e125be
greek_terms:
  - στάσις
  - ταὐτόν
  - θάτερον
  - μεταβάλλειν
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The Stranger argues that Same and Other must be distinct kinds from Motion and Rest because a common predicate of both cannot be identified with either. If Same or Other were one of Motion or Rest, whichever received the attribute would force the other to change into the opposite of its own nature: Motion would stand still and Rest would move."
textual_basis: "At 254e-255a the Stranger states that Motion and Rest are neither Other nor Same, because whatever is said in common of both Motion and Rest cannot be either of them. The argument at 255a shows the absurd consequence: if Other applied to both, whichever of Motion or Rest received it would force the other to change to its opposite nature."
limits: This observation records the argument form but does not evaluate whether the argument successfully establishes Same and Other as distinct kinds. It does not address whether the five kinds form a complete set.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
  - τὸ ταὐτὸν

observation: |
  The Stranger uses a reductio argument: if Being and the Same signified nothing different, then since both Motion and Rest are called beings, they would also have to be called "the same" in that respect — which Theaetetus agrees is impossible.
textual_basis: |
  At 255b-255c, the Stranger hypothesizes that Being and the Same signify one thing, then draws the consequence that when one says both Motion and Rest are beings, one would also be saying both are the same insofar as they are beings. Theaetetus responds that this is impossible (adunaton).
limits: |
  This observation records the argument form. It does not assess whether the reductio succeeds on grounds beyond what the text asserts, nor does it claim that this is the only or primary argument for separating Same from Being.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0210

  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: Theaetetus's brief assent at 252a triggers the Eleatic Visitor to declare that all previous positions about being are now overturned by the shared admission that being is attributed to everything.
textual_basis: "At 252a, after Theaetetus gives a one-word negative answer, the Visitor immediately responds that by this agreement everything has been overthrown. The visitor then lists three groups: those who move the whole, those who make it one and stationary, and those who say beings are by forms always the same. All these groups, he says, attach being to their accounts, so the single admission that being is not non-participating collapses all prior ontological positions."
limits: This observation does not assess whether the visitor's inference is valid; it only records the textual structure where a short assent triggers a sweeping dialectical consequence.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

greek_terms:
  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The Eleatic Visitor enumerates three identifiable groups of thinkers about being at 252a: those who say everything is really in motion, those who say things are really at rest, and those who say beings are by forms always the same. A fourth group, those who combine and divide, is added at 252b."
textual_basis: At 252a the Visitor explicitly names the group of those who move the whole, those who make things one and stationary, and those who say beings are by forms always the same. At 252b he adds those who sometimes compose all things and sometimes divide them. The enumeration is a roll-call of pre-Sophist ontological positions that the visitor will use to demonstrate the necessity of combination.
limits: This observation does not identify which historical thinkers correspond to each group. It only records that the text enumerates them as dramatic adversaries whose positions are being tested.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
  - σύμμειξις
english_gloss: ""
observation: At 252b the Visitor states that if there is no mixture at all, then all the enumerated positions would say nothing, and moreover those who forbid calling one thing by another's affection would themselves be the most ridiculous practitioners of discourse.
textual_basis: "At 252b the Visitor declares that in all these ways they would say nothing if there is no mixture, and then adds that those who allow no communion of one thing with another's affection would themselves most ridiculously pursue discourse. The passage thus shows that the denial of combination is self-refuting: anyone who speaks to deny combination must already use combination in speaking."
limits: "This observation does not claim that the visitor is making an esoteric argument about the nature of being. It records the explicit textual structure of a universal refutation: if there is no mixture, all prior positions collapse, and the denial of mixture itself becomes absurd because language requires mixture."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0213
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 256e-257a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 256e-257a
  start_marker: 256e
  end_marker: 257a
  start_char: 80062
  end_char: 80807
  text_sha256: 2edebb8a2941102d136acb4ddf3d7adc3a9218ba11a9543d6a3aa82ded45b283
greek_terms:
  - τὸ ὂν αὐτὸ
  - τῶν ἄλλων ἕτερον
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Visitor asserts that Being itself must be said to be other than the other kinds, and that Being is not in as many respects as the other kinds are (257a). This treats Being as one member of a network of kinds whose not-being is determined relationally by difference, rather than as a unique overarching principle exempt from otherness.
textual_basis: At 257a the Visitor states that Being itself must be said to be other than the others (to on auto ton allon heteron einai lekteon) and follows immediately with the claim that Being is not in as many respects as the others are, since Being is one thing itself while the others, unlimited in number, are not in turn.
limits: This observation records the relational placement of Being within the communion of kinds as stated in the text. It does not claim that Plato abandons any transcendent role for Being, nor does it assert that the text resolves how Being can both be one kind among others and the ground of participation in being.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0214
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 256e-257a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 256e-257a
  start_marker: 256e
  end_marker: 257a
  start_char: 80062
  end_char: 80807
  text_sha256: 2edebb8a2941102d136acb4ddf3d7adc3a9218ba11a9543d6a3aa82ded45b283
greek_terms:
  - τοὺς ἔμπροσθεν λόγους
  - πειθέτω τὰ μετὰ ταῦτα
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The Visitor sets an explicit procedural condition for disputing the conclusions: anyone who does not concede the account of the communion of kinds must first refute the previous arguments before challenging what follows (257a)."
textual_basis: At 257a the Visitor says that if anyone does not concede these things, let that person first persuade (refute) our previous arguments and then persuade the ones that follow (peisas hemon tous emprosthen logous houto peithetō ta meta tauta). Theaetetus endorses this as most just (dikaiotata).
limits: This is a rhetorical framing device within the dialogue. It does not establish that the arguments are in fact irrefutable, nor does it provide evidence about Plato's actual dialectical practice outside the text.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0215
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 256e-257a

  text_sha256: 2edebb8a2941102d136acb4ddf3d7adc3a9218ba11a9543d6a3aa82ded45b283
greek_terms:
  - ἄπειρον
  - πλήθει
  - τὸ μὴ ὄν
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Visitor claims that relative to each form, being is many (polu) while not-being is unlimited in multitude (apeiron plethei, 256e-257a). Not-being is thus characterized as quantitatively unbounded once defined relationally through otherness.
textual_basis: "At 256e-257a, immediately after restating that the nature of the different makes each thing a not-being relative to being, the Visitor draws the consequence: concerning each of the forms, being is many but not-being is unlimited in multitude (peri hekaston ara ton eidon polu men esti to on, apeiron de plethei to me on)."
limits: The claim about unlimited multitude is asserted as a consequence of the relational definition of not-being but is not demonstrated by enumeration. The observation records the asserted characterization without evaluating its validity.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0216
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 257b-257c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 257b-257c
  start_marker: 257b
  end_marker: 257c
  start_char: 80807
  end_char: 81613
  text_sha256: 2447a9587fcc322f9bf4bee775c7600935bebfcfcf535b9befb0c0c089f9f305
greek_terms:
  - τὸ μὴ ὂν
  - ἐναντίον
  - ἕτερον
  - ἀπόφασις
  - τὸ μὴ
  - τὸ οὒ
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The Eleatic Stranger argues that negation (apophasis) does not signify contrariety (enantion). When we say 'not-being' (to me on), we do not say something contrary to being but only something different (heteron). He generalizes: the negative particles 'not' (to me) and 'no' (to ou), when prefixed to names, indicate something among 'the other things' (ton allon), not contraries."
textual_basis: At 257b, the Stranger states that 'not-being' is not the contrary of being but only different (heteron monon). He illustrates with 'not large' (me mega), which does not indicate 'the small' (the contrary) but could indicate 'the equal.' At 257b-257c, he concludes that negation does not signify contrariety but indicates something of the other things.
limits: This observation records the claim as stated in the text. It does not assess whether the argument succeeds, how it relates to Parmenidean prohibitions on not-being, or whether the analysis covers all uses of negation in Plato.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0217
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 257b-257c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 257b-257c
  start_marker: 257b
  end_marker: 257c
  start_char: 80807
  end_char: 81613
  text_sha256: 2447a9587fcc322f9bf4bee775c7600935bebfcfcf535b9befb0c0c089f9f305
greek_terms:
  - κατακεκερματίσθαι
  - ἐπιστήμη
  - ἐπί τῳ
  - ἀφορισθὲν
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Stranger claims that the nature of the other (he thaterou physis) is fragmented or cut up (katakekermatisthai) just like knowledge (episteme). Knowledge is one, but each part that is about something (epi toi) and marked off (aphoristhen) receives its own name.
textual_basis: "At 257c, the Stranger states: 'The nature of the other appears to me to be cut up just like knowledge.' He explains that knowledge is one, but each part arising in relation to some subject and marked off gets its own name. This maps the source domain (knowledge and its parts) onto the target domain (the nature of the other)."
limits: This observation records the analogy as stated. It does not evaluate whether the analogy holds, how it connects to the preceding analysis of negation, or how the partitioning of the other relates to the kinds discussed elsewhere in the dialogue.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
  - ὁμοίως
  - τί μήν
  - πῶς γὰρ οὔ
  - σαφέστατά γε
english_gloss: ""
observation: Theaetetus responds to the Eleatic Stranger's successive propositions with four brief affirmative answers in an unbroken chain at 258a-258b.
textual_basis: "Theaetetus's replies are all short assent formulas: similarly, of course, how could it not be, and most clearly. Each occurs immediately after a Stranger question or proposition about the status of not-being and the nature of the Different."
limits: This observation records only the pattern of short assents. It does not claim that Theaetetus is coerced, that his assent is insincere, or that the reasoning chain is valid.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0219

greek_terms:
  - ἐναντίον
  - ἕτερον
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Eleatic Stranger draws an explicit distinction at 258b between the opposition of not-being to being as signifying otherness rather than contrariety.
textual_basis: 'At 258b the Stranger states that the opposition of the part of the Different to being signifies not the contrary of it but only so much: other than it. The Stranger marks the statement as bold with the hedge "if it is lawful to say."'
limits: This observation records the textual distinction between contrary and other. It does not evaluate whether the distinction succeeds against Parmenides or whether it is original to Plato.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
textual_basis: When the Stranger asks what then shall we call it, Theaetetus answers that clearly, what is not, which they were seeking because of the sophist, is this very thing.
limits: This observation records the identification of the inquiry target within the dialogue. It does not claim that the identification resolves the inquiry or that the sophist has been captured.
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0221
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 257d-257e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 257d-257e
  start_marker: 257d
  end_marker: 257e
  start_char: 81613
  end_char: 82435
  text_sha256: c39e1ca6408ec998de0a2d58fa26fbe04e86541821f23014f586d810de9052e8
greek_terms:
  - τὸ μὴ καλόν
  - θατέρου μόριον
  - ἀντίθεσις
english_gloss: the not-beautiful as a part of the other opposed to the beautiful
observation: "At 257d-257e, the Eleatic Stranger analyzes 'the not-beautiful' (to me kalon) not as a privation or non-being but as a part (morion) of the nature of the Different (thaterou physis) set in opposition (antithesis) to the beautiful. He asks whether there is a part of the Different opposed to the beautiful, and Theaetetus confirms that 'the not-beautiful' is nothing other than something different from the nature of the beautiful (heteron estin e tes tou kalou physeos). At 257e the Stranger reframes this: the not-beautiful comes to be when something is marked off (aphoristhen) from one single kind among beings and set in opposition (antitithen) to something among beings — so it is a being-against-being opposition (ontos pros on antithesis)."
textual_basis: "The Stranger explicitly poses the question at 257d: 'Is there a part of the Different opposed to the beautiful?' (esti to kalo ti thaterou morion antitithemenon). Theaetetus names the negation at 257d: 'what we call the not-beautiful is nothing other than different from the nature of the beautiful.' At 257e the Stranger introduces the formula of opposition: something marked off from one kind of beings and set against another being, concluding that the not-beautiful turns out to be 'an opposition of being to being' (ontos pros on antithesis)."
limits: This observation records the Stranger's positive analysis of negation as a part of the Different. It does not address whether this analysis succeeds against Parmenides' prohibition on not-being. The scope is limited to the not-beautiful example; the generalization to other negations is implicit but not yet tested here.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0222
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 257d-257e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 257d-257e
  start_marker: 257d
  end_marker: 257e
  start_char: 81613
  end_char: 82435
  text_sha256: c39e1ca6408ec998de0a2d58fa26fbe04e86541821f23014f586d810de9052e8
greek_terms:
  - ἀνώνυμον
  - ἐπωνυμίαν
english_gloss: nameless versus having a name for a part of the Different
observation: "At 257d, after Theaetetus agrees that there is a part of the Different opposed to the beautiful, the Stranger asks whether this part is nameless (anonymon) or has some name (eponymian). Theaetetus answers that it does have a name: 'what we call the not-beautiful' (ho gar me kalon hekastote phthengometha). This reveals an asymmetry in the language of negation: positive forms like 'beautiful' have dedicated names, while their negations are named only by the compound 'not-X' — yet the Stranger treats this compound as a genuine name for a part of the Different."
textual_basis: "At 257d the Stranger asks: 'Shall we say this is nameless or has some name?' (tout' oun anonymon eroumen e tin' echon eponymian). Theaetetus replies: 'It has one — for what we call the not-beautiful each time, this is nothing other than different from the nature of the beautiful.' The Stranger's question and Theaetetus's answer together register that the negation's name is derivative (a 'not-X' compound) yet functionally serves as a name for a distinct part of the Different."
limits: This records the naming asymmetry for the not-beautiful case only. It does not claim that all negations have this naming structure, nor does it analyze why some parts of the Different lack dedicated positive names.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0223

greek_terms:
  - πέντε
  - γένη
english_gloss: ""
observation: |
  The Eleatic Stranger invokes the five greatest kinds as an already-established
  framework for argument, using their agreed-upon number and identities to
  structure the deduction that not-being exists through the nature of the other.
textual_basis: |
  At 256d the Stranger says "having agreed they are five, concerning which and
  among which we proposed to investigate" (256d). The whole argument in
  256c-256d operates by positioning motion relative to the five kinds — being,
  motion, rest, same, and other — without re-arguing their enumeration.
limits: |
  The passage does not enumerate or derive the five kinds; it takes them as
  previously established and uses them as an argumentative frame.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

  - θατέρου
english_gloss: ""
observation: |
  The Stranger concludes that not-being exists of necessity, both in the case
  of motion and across all the kinds, through the nature of the other. This
  establishes not-being as a necessary feature of the local ontological
  framework.
textual_basis: |
  At 256d the Stranger states that "not-being exists of necessity both in the
  case of motion and according to all the kinds; for in all of them the nature
  of the other..." after Theaetetus agrees that the number of five kinds cannot
  be reduced and the Stranger restates that motion is other than being while
  participating in being.
limits: |
  The passage establishes the existence of not-being as otherness but does not
  exhaustively work out the implications for each individual kind. It does not
  address whether not-being is itself a distinct kind.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0225
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 258e-259a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 258e-259a
  start_marker: 258e
  end_marker: 259a
  start_char: 84104
  end_char: 84957
  text_sha256: 69aebdb191cc2a148613a41404ffaeccfecafef7daa63bd79b213876c3e4a2b9
greek_terms:
  - τοὐναντίον
  - τὸ μὴ ὄν
  - ἐναντίου
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Eleatic Stranger preemptively rejects an anticipated objection — that the account of not-being he has given amounts to claiming that not-being is the contrary of being, and that this contrary exists. He dismisses this reading before any interlocutor raises it.
textual_basis: At 258e-259a the Stranger says "let no one say that we, declaring not-being to be the contrary of being, dare to say that it is" (me toinun hemas eipe tis hoti tounantion tou ontos to me on apophainomenoi tolmomen legein hos estin). He then states they have long dismissed any contrary of being, whether it exists or not, whether it has an account or is entirely without one.
limits: This records the preemptive objection-rejection structure. It does not evaluate whether the distinction between contrary and negation is philosophically successful, nor does it claim the Stranger is correct.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0226

greek_terms:
  - ἐλέγξας
  - λεκτέον
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The Stranger issues a formal refutation challenge to any objector: either refute the account of not-being and persuade them they speak incorrectly, or, while unable to do so, the objector must say the same as they do."
textual_basis: At 259a the Stranger states "what we have now said not-being is — either let someone persuade us that we speak incorrectly by refuting us, or as long as he is unable, he too must say as we say" (e peisato tis hos ou kalos legomen elenxas, e mechriper an adynate, lekteon kai ekeino kathaper hemeis legomen).
limits: This records the refutation-challenge procedure. It does not evaluate whether the Stranger's argument withstands refutation, nor does it claim the challenge is sincere or merely rhetorical.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0227
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 258e-259a

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt

  text_sha256: 69aebdb191cc2a148613a41404ffaeccfecafef7daa63bd79b213876c3e4a2b9
greek_terms:
  - τὰ γένη
  - θάτερον
  - μέθεξιν
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The Stranger states a positive metaphysical doctrine: the kinds mix with each other, being and otherness go through everything and through each other, and otherness — by partaking of being — IS, but is not that of which it partakes, but rather other; and being other than being, it most clearly IS."
textual_basis: At 259a the Stranger declares "the kinds mix with each other, and being and otherness go through everything and through each other, and otherness, partaking of being, IS because of this participation, but is not that of which it partook but other, and being other than being, it most clearly IS" (symmeignytai te allelois ta gene kai to te on kai thateron dia panton kai di allelon dielelythote to men heteron metaschon tou ontos esti men dia tauten ten methexin, ou men ekeino ge hou meteschen all' heteron, heteron de tou ontos on esti saphestata).
limits: This records the assertion of the kinds-mixing doctrine. It does not evaluate whether the doctrine successfully resolves the problem of not-being, nor does it assess the coherence of the participation claim.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0228

observation: The Eleatic Stranger identifies not-being as a single form enumerated among the many beings and specifies that this form is the nature of the Other.
textual_basis: At 258c the Stranger states that not-being is enumerated as a single form among the many beings. At 258d he says they have made manifest the form that not-being happens to be and that they demonstrated the nature of the Other as being.
limits: This observation records the identification of not-being with the form of the Other. It does not assess whether this refutes Parmenides or whether the argument for this identification succeeds.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0229

greek_terms:
  - οὐ γὰρ μήποτε τοῦτο δαμῇ, εἶναι μὴ ἐόντα
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Eleatic Stranger explicitly quotes Parmenides' prohibition against saying that not-being is and claims that he and Theaetetus have gone beyond Parmenides' prohibition, demonstrating not only that not-being is but also what form it has.
textual_basis: At 258c-258d the Stranger quotes Parmenides' verse prohibition directly, then states at 258d that they have not only demonstrated that things that are not are but also made manifest the form that not-being happens to be. He frames this as going further than Parmenides forbade.
limits: This observation records the explicit contradiction of Parmenides' prohibition. It does not evaluate whether the Stranger's argument actually violates or respects Parmenides' position.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0231

greek_terms:
  - ἐπιστήμην
  - ἐλευθέρων
  - φιλόσοφον
  - σοφιστὴν
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Stranger asks what name to give this knowledge — the science of how kinds blend or divide — and suggests they may have stumbled upon the knowledge of free men, and in seeking the sophist may have found the philosopher first.
textual_basis: At 253c, the Stranger asks what they shall now call this, then exclaims they may have fallen into the knowledge belonging to free men and that in hunting the sophist they may have first discovered the philosopher.
limits: This observation records the identification of the dialectical knowledge with the philosopher's domain. It does not analyze what distinguishes the philosopher from the sophist, nor does it assess whether the Stranger is being playful or serious.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0232
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 256a-256b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 256a-256b
  start_marker: 256a
  end_marker: 256b
  start_char: 78333
  end_char: 79177
  text_sha256: d696e2b2528b514697d350b6520cf2670b756178c88c6af14dba988eff10b878
greek_terms:
  - ταὐτόν
  - μέθεξιν
  - κοινωνίαν
english_gloss: ""
observation: |
  The Eleatic Stranger explains that Motion can correctly be called both "the same" and "not the same" by distinguishing two relations: it is called "the same" through its participation in the Same in relation to itself, and "not the same" through its community with the Other, which separates it from the Same and makes it different. The apparent contradiction is resolved not by denying either predicate but by tracing each to participation in a distinct Form.
textual_basis: |
  At 256a-256b the Stranger states that when we say Motion is "the same" we say so through its participation in the Same in relation to itself (dia ten methexin tautou pros heauten), and when we say it is "not the same" we say so through its community with the Other (dia ten koinonian thaterou), which separates it from the Same. He insists one must not be troubled by saying Motion both is and is not the same (ou duscheranteon), because the two predications are made in different respects.
limits: |
  This observation does not claim that the Stranger's distinction solves all problems of contradictory predication. It only notes the textual move of resolving the apparent contradiction by appeal to distinct participations in different Forms.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0233
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 256a-256b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 256a-256b
  start_marker: 256a
  end_marker: 256b
  start_char: 78333
  end_char: 79177
  text_sha256: d696e2b2528b514697d350b6520cf2670b756178c88c6af14dba988eff10b878
greek_terms:
  - κίνησις
  - στάσεως
  - στάσιμον
english_gloss: ""
observation: |
  The Eleatic Stranger introduces a counterfactual hypothetical: if Motion partook of Rest in any way, it would not be absurd to call it stationary. The hypothetical is used to test the principle that some kinds are willing to mix with one another while others are not, which Theaetetus endorses as a condition for accepting the hypothetical.
textual_basis: |
  At 256b the Stranger asks (using an ei-clause counterfactual) whether, if Motion partook of Rest in some respect, it would be at all absurd to call it stationary. Theaetetus replies that this would be most correct, provided they agree that some kinds are willing to mix with one another and others are not (eiper ton genon sunchoresometha ta men allelois ethelein meignusthai, ta de me).
limits: |
  This observation notes the hypothetical form and its conditional acceptance by the respondent. It does not assess whether the hypothetical is subsequently developed or whether Motion in fact partakes of Rest.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: The Stranger synthesizes not-being's mixture with thought and speech into the possibility of falsity, deception, images, and appearances.
textual_basis: At 260c-260d the conditional chain links not-being, false opinion and speech, deception, and the sophist's refuge in image-making.
limits: This does not prove the final sophist classification; it records the synthesis that makes that return possible.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0235
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 260d

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt

  end_char: 88212
  text_sha256: e80a6d7c618d8d01879c439eed279f55495f236f9a31d81908cef7130f73624d
greek_terms:
  - τάχα δ’ ἂν φαίη
  - λόγον
  - δόξαν
  - εἰδωλοποιικὴν
  - φανταστικήν
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The Stranger anticipates and voices a possible counter-move by the absent sophist: conceding that some forms participate in not-being while others do not, and claiming that speech and opinion belong to those that do not, so that the image-making and appearance-producing art (where the sophist was placed) would remain untouched by the refutation."
textual_basis: At 260d the Stranger says that perhaps the sophist would claim that some of the forms participate in not-being and others do not, and that speech and opinion are among those that do not participate, so the image-making and appearance-producing art in which they said the sophist is would be safe. The Stranger speaks the sophist's hypothetical objection in the sophist's voice.
limits: This observation does not assess whether the ventriloquized objection is plausible or whether the Stranger's subsequent response to it is adequate. It only notes the textual device of the speaker voicing an absent opponent's anticipated argument.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0236
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 259b-259c

  text_sha256: f0bc624d9d241e200e276545f963d30936ba2347b68facf562a0f7e13e68333c
greek_terms:
  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: At 259b-c, the Eleatic Stranger states that anyone who disbelieves the oppositions just established must examine the matter and say something better than what has now been said; if instead someone delights in dragging arguments now this way now that way, he has not exerted effort worthy of serious attention.
textual_basis: "The Stranger uses an explicit conditional structure (eite... eite...) that distinguishes two respondent postures: genuine examination (skepteon auto kai lekteon beltion ti) versus eristic play (chairei... tous logous helkon). This is a procedural directive framing how the interlocutor should continue."
limits: The observation does not assess whether Theaetetus follows this instruction, nor does it claim the Stranger is accusing Theaetetus specifically of eristic play.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0237
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 259b-259c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt

  start_marker: 259b

  end_char: 85836
  text_sha256: f0bc624d9d241e200e276545f963d30936ba2347b68facf562a0f7e13e68333c
greek_terms:
  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The Eleatic Stranger at 259b-c contrasts two attitudes toward the dialectical results: serious examination aimed at improvement, versus delight in pulling arguments back and forth. He dismisses the latter as not worth much serious effort (ouk axia polles spoudes espoudaken), then contrasts it with what is genuinely difficult and fine (chalepon hama kai kalon)."
textual_basis: "The Stranger explicitly uses spoude/play vocabulary: ouk axia polles spoudes espoudaken for eristic dragging, followed immediately by an elevation of what is chalepon kai kalon. This maps onto the recurring pattern where speakers frame a dialectical move as either serious or mere play."
limits: The observation registers the seriousness/play contrast as a textual device. It does not determine whether the Stranger is being ironic or sincere in this dismissal.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
textual_basis: Theaetetus's turn is a single interrogative phrase (to poion;) that follows the Stranger's distinction between the easy/eristic and the difficult/noble task. This is a standard respondent turn that invites elaboration from the questioner.
limits: This observation only records the turn-taking pattern. It does not evaluate whether Theaetetus's question is strategically motivated or naive.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0239

  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Eleatic Stranger uses a proverbial saying about 'taking a city' to encourage Theaetetus not to lose heart in the inquiry, likening the completion of one argumentative task to capturing the greatest wall, after which the remaining tasks will be easier.
textual_basis: At 261c the Stranger says that according to the proverb someone who loses heart would hardly take a city; he then declares that the greatest wall has been taken and the rest will be easier and shorter (261c).
limits: The observation records the procedural use of a proverb to manage interlocutor morale; it does not analyze the content of any argument about not-being or falsehood.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0240

greek_terms:

english_gloss: ""
observation: "The Eleatic Stranger signals that the previous argumentative task has been completed and announces the next step: to take up speech (logos) and opinion (doxa) in order to determine whether not-being touches them or whether both are entirely true and never false."
textual_basis: At 261b Theaetetus notes that the task of demonstrating that falsehood exists in speech and opinion has been proposed and there seems no end; at 261c the Stranger responds that the greatest wall is taken and now they must take up logos and doxa to examine their relation to not-being (261c).
limits: This observation records a procedural recapitulation and forward-looking step announcement. It does not evaluate whether the argument about not-being is correct or complete.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
textual_basis: Theaetetus responds with 'well said' (261c) to the Stranger's encouragement and with 'correctly' (261c) to the proposal to take up logos and doxa, giving the inquiry explicit respondent endorsement.
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0242

greek_terms:
  - λόγον
  - διομολογήσασθαι
english_gloss: speech/discourse; one kind of the things that are; to agree on a definition
observation: The Eleatic Visitor frames the inquiry as needing to determine what sort of thing logos is among the kinds of being. The passage states that logos is one kind of the things that are and that failing to secure this would deprive them of philosophy.
textual_basis: "At 260a, the Visitor connects the previous battle over mixing to a pressing question: what logos is and why it must be established as a kind of being. The immediate imperative to agree on a definition of logos is stated at 260a-260b, with the consequence that without this, they could not speak at all."
limits: This observation does not claim that the definition is given in this segment. The inquiry target is marked but the actual definition of logos is deferred.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0243

greek_terms:
  - μείγνυται
  - δόξῃ
  - λόγῳ
english_gloss: not-being; mixes; judgment; speech
observation: The Visitor frames the next step of the inquiry as a test of whether not-being mixes with judgment and speech. The procedure moves from identifying that not-being is a kind scattered through all things to checking its mixture with specific cognitive items.
textual_basis: "At 260b, after summarizing that not-being was shown to be one dispersed kind, the Visitor immediately proposes the conditional test: whether not-being mixes with judgment and speech. The next step is explicitly made dependent on the prior finding about not-being."
limits: This observation does not claim that the test is completed here; the segment merely sets up the hypothesis for examination. The actual examination proceeds later.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0244

greek_terms:
  - στερηθεῖμεν
  - φιλοσοφίας
  - μηδεμίαν
  - μεῖξιν
english_gloss: we would be deprived; of philosophy; no; mixture
observation: The Visitor marks a transition from the earlier battle over mixture to the present inquiry about logos by recalling the stakes of that battle. At 260a he says they were fighting in that earlier exchange so that logos would be one kind of being, and that if they had conceded no mixture at all, they would be deprived of philosophy and of the ability to speak.
textual_basis: "At 260a-260b, the Visitor explicitly recaps the previous dispute and then states the counterfactual consequence: if all mixture were denied, logos itself would be impossible. The passage connects the completed argument about mixture to the new task with a clear transitional move."
limits: This observation identifies the transitional structure of the passage. It does not assess whether the earlier argument about mixture was successful or what the full implications are.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0245
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 259d-259e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 259d-259e
  start_marker: 259d
  end_marker: 259e
  start_char: 85836
  end_char: 86515
  text_sha256: 3a53a3b030a3b4e1ebc7bfae490dce70ecaaa26ce419a86bd1f1cee0e7ac57e5
greek_terms:
  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Eleatic Stranger distinguishes the practice of showing that the same is other or the other same, and delighting in always bringing forward contraries in arguments, from true refutation (elenchos). He calls this kind of argumentative practice the activity of someone who has just recently laid hold of something that is, i.e., a newcomer.
textual_basis: At 259d, the Stranger says that proclaiming the same as other in some respect and the other as same, and rejoicing in always producing contraries in logoi, is not a genuine refutation (outis elenchos houtos alethinos) but is manifestly the work of someone new-born who has just touched upon something that is (arti te ton onton tinos ephaptomenou delos neogenes on).
limits: The observation establishes that the Stranger explicitly contrasts a mere verbal manipulation of contraries with authentic refutation. It does not claim that any specific sophist is being named, nor does it draw conclusions about what constitutes genuine refutation.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation: Separating each thing from all destroys discourse because logos depends on the interweaving of forms.
textual_basis: At 259d-259e the Stranger gives the interweaving of forms as the reason total separation would remove discourse.
limits: This records the condition for logos, not one-over-many reasoning.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0247

greek_terms:
  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Stranger characterizes the person who attempts to separate everything from everything as belonging to someone unmusical (amousos) and unphilosophical (aphilosophos). This creates a negative ethical type defined by the refusal to recognize the interweaving of forms.
textual_basis: At 259d-259e, the Stranger says that attempting to separate everything from everything is not fitting (ouk emmeles) and indeed is characteristic of someone completely unmusical and unphilosophical (pantapasin amousou tinos kai aphilosophou).
limits: The passage characterizes a stance or practice, not a named individual. It does not claim that any particular historical figure holds this position.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0248
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 262a-262b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 262a-262b
  start_marker: 262a
  end_marker: 262b
  start_char: 90467
  end_char: 91309
  text_sha256: 21988a6bb83239747d2b23a21db312506784b594421babda090df565a0e88db1
greek_terms:
  - ὀνόματα
  - ῥήματα
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The Eleatic Stranger divides vocal signs into two kinds: names, which are signs applied to the agents of actions, and verbs, which are signs that indicate actions."
textual_basis: At 262a the Stranger states that the indication concerning actions is called a verb, while the vocal sign applied to the agents performing those actions is called a name. The Stranger marks this as a bipartite distinction with a 'the one...the other...' construction.
limits: This is a classification of linguistic items within the Stranger's diairetic analysis of statement; it does not yet address whether this division is exhaustive or how names and verbs combine.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0249
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 262a-262b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 262a-262b
  start_marker: 262a
  end_marker: 262b
  start_char: 90467
  end_char: 91309
  text_sha256: 21988a6bb83239747d2b23a21db312506784b594421babda090df565a0e88db1
greek_terms:
  - λόγος
  - ὀνόματα
  - ῥήματα
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The Stranger proposes a minimal combination condition for statement: a continuous sequence of names alone or of verbs alone never constitutes a statement. The Stranger illustrates with verb-only examples (walks, runs, sleeps) and name-only examples (lion, deer, horse), none of which produce a statement."
textual_basis: At 262a the Stranger asserts that from names alone spoken in succession there is never a statement, nor from verbs spoken apart from names. At 262b he clarifies with examples showing that even an exhaustive sequential list of verbs or names produces no statement at all.
limits: This observation records the combinatorial criterion for statement being stated; it does not claim that the Stranger has yet provided a full definition of statement, which continues in the subsequent lines.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
textual_basis: The Stranger says 'just as we were saying about the forms and the letters, let us examine the names again in the same way' (261d), making the prior discussion an explicit template for the current inquiry.
limits: Does not establish what the earlier discussion of forms and letters concluded; only records that it is invoked as a methodological precedent.
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0251

  - συναρμόττει
  - ἐθέλει
english_gloss: ""
observation: At 261d, the Stranger poses a tripartite question about whether all names fit together with each other, none do, or some do and some do not. Theaetetus immediately selects the third option without hesitation or argument.
textual_basis: "The Stranger asks 'whether all fit together with each other, or none, or some are willing and some not,' and Theaetetus answers 'this is clear: some are willing, some not' (261d). The Stranger then clarifies the criterion: things said in sequence that indicate something fit together, while those that signify nothing in the sequence do not (261d-261e)."
limits: Does not establish whether Theaetetus's quick assent is correct or naive; only records the structure of the exchange and the clarification that follows.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
textual_basis: The Stranger states 'there is for us, I suppose, a twofold kind of the vocal indicators concerning being' (261e). This announcement immediately follows the clarification about which names fit together and which do not.
limits: Does not specify what the two kinds are; the distinction itself falls in the next segment. Only records the announcement of a bifurcation as a methodological setup.
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0253

source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 261a
  start_marker: 261a
  end_marker: 261a
  start_char: 88506
  end_char: 89004
  text_sha256: 21541b8f9148a5cb243e698dc489ac2f44caab186d757f40061bd8affa737b63
greek_terms:
  - δυσθήρευτον
  - προβλημάτων γέμειν
  - διαμάχεσθαι
  - μὴ ὂν
  - ψεῦδος
english_gloss: ""
observation: |
  Theaetetus observes that the sophist's genus is hard to hunt and that it
  is loaded with problems; when one problem is thrown forward, it is
  necessary to fight through it before reaching the sophist himself. He
  remarks that they have barely gotten through the earlier problem that
  not-being is not, and now another problem has been thrown forward.
textual_basis: |
  At 261a Theaetetus reflects on the procedure: the sophist genus appears
  to be full of preliminary problems, and each one must be fought through
  before advancing. He notes that they have barely crossed the earlier
  problem about not-being and that another problem has now been thrown
  forward. The passage makes explicit that the progression is a series of
  preliminary battles.
limits: |
  Does not claim that the dialogue is secretly about something else. The
  observation notes only the explicit procedural remark about the need to
  fight through preliminary problems before reaching the target.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0255
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 262e-263a

  text_sha256: 5cb3b3bf89d029cc3d0c0aa95ae15b3df171235288f7dceb203c63b69a89164e
greek_terms:
  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Eleatic Stranger invites Theaetetus to identify the subject of each constructed logos. For 'Theaetetus sits', Theaetetus answers it is about himself and of himself (peri emou te kai emos). For 'Theaetetus flies', Theaetetus says no one could say otherwise than that it is of him and about him. The Stranger then asks what quality (poion) each logos necessarily has.
textual_basis: "At 262e the Stranger says: 'I will speak a logos to you, combining a thing with an action through a noun and a verb; and you tell me what the logos is of' (hotou d' an ho logos e, su moi phrazein). At 263a Theaetetus performs this identification for both examples, and the Stranger immediately shifts to the question of quality (poion de ge tina phamen anankaion hekaston einai ton logon)."
limits: This observation records the pedagogical-dialectical procedure of testing a definition through constructed examples and respondent identification. It does not analyze the truth or falsity of the constructed logoi.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0256

greek_terms:
  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The Stranger assigns Theaetetus an active identificatory task (su moi phrazein) and Theaetetus performs it twice: first identifying the logos as about himself, then confirming the second logos is also about himself. The Stranger explicitly marks the small remaining step (eti de smikron tode) before proceeding. Theaetetus's first response limits itself to what is within his power (kata dunamin)."
textual_basis: "At 262e the Stranger says 'you tell me' (su moi phrazein) and Theaetetus replies 'this will be according to my ability' (taut' estai kata dunamin). At 263a Theaetetus answers both test cases: 'clearly about me and mine' (delon hoti peri emou te kai emos) and 'no one could say otherwise than mine and about me' (oud' an heis allos eipoi plen emon te kai peri emou)."
limits: This observation records the turn-taking structure with task assignment and respondent compliance. It does not evaluate whether Theaetetus's answers are correct or assess the definitional adequacy of the test.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0257

greek_terms:
  - ψευδὴς
  - ἀληθὴς
  - ὄντα
english_gloss: ""
observation: |
  The Eleatic Stranger and Theaetetus jointly establish a bipartite definition of true and false speech at 263b: true speech says things that are as they are, while false speech says things that are not as though they are things that are.
textual_basis: |
  At 263b the Stranger asks Theaetetus to classify each kind of speech. Theaetetus answers that one is false, the other true. The Stranger then explicates: true speech says the things that are as they are about you, and false speech says things other than the things that are, treating what is not as though it is.
limits: |
  The passage defines the truth/falsity of speech through the ontological categories of being and not-being, not through correspondence to fact or perceptual verification. The definition is stated as a preliminary framing device for the subsequent analysis of logos structure rather than as the dialogue's final account.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0259
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 264a-264b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 264a-264b
  start_marker: 264a
  end_marker: 264b
  start_char: 94746
  end_char: 95559
  text_sha256: fda5e1eb0164ca830b52ad22ac6618dec4ca8201f76d34b299f971e4adb10b3e
greek_terms:
  - διάνοια
  - δόξα
  - φαντασία
  - λόγος
  - διάλογος
  - σύμμειξις
english_gloss: ""
observation: At 264a-264b, the Eleatic Visitor consolidates a chain of definitions that links thought (dianoia) to silent inner dialogue of the soul with itself, opinion (doxa) to the completion of thought, appearance (phantasia) to a mixture of perception and opinion, and then insists that since speech (logos) can be true or false and these inner items are akin to speech, some of them must also sometimes be false.
textual_basis: "The Visitor asks Theaetetus what to call thinking that occurs in the soul with silence apart from opinion (264a), then what to call the same affection when it comes not by itself but through perception, securing \"phantasia\" as the only correct term. He then states the chain explicitly: logos was shown true and false; dianoia is the soul's dialogue with itself, doxa is the completion of dianoia, and what \"we call phainetai\" is a mixture of perception and doxa (264a-264b). From the kinship of these to logos, he concludes that some of them must be false."
limits: This observation records the compressed definitional chain consolidating earlier distinctions into a single argumentative move. It does not assess the validity of the kinship argument or whether the definitions of dianoia and doxa given here are novel.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

greek_terms:
  - ψευδὴς δόξα
  - προσδοκία
  - ἀνήνυτον ἔργον
english_gloss: ""
observation: At 264b, the Visitor marks a closure point in the inquiry by noting that false opinion and false speech were discovered earlier than expected and earlier than the fear expressed earlier that the search for them would be an endless task. He then exhorts Theaetetus not to lose heart for what remains.
textual_basis: The Visitor explicitly recalls the prior expectation-driven fear about pursuing false opinion and speech being an endless task (264b), contrasts it with the present discovery, and uses this to pivot toward the remaining inquiry with a motivational exhortation.
limits: This observation records the closure-and-transition rhetorical structure. It does not evaluate whether false opinion and speech have actually been adequately demonstrated or whether the remaining inquiry will succeed.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0261
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 264a-264b

  text_sha256: fda5e1eb0164ca830b52ad22ac6618dec4ca8201f76d34b299f971e4adb10b3e
greek_terms:
  - διάνοια
  - ψυχῆς διάλογος
  - δόξα
  - διανοίας ἀποτελεύτησις
  - φαίνεται
  - σύμμειξις
english_gloss: ""
observation: "At 264a-264b, the Visitor proposes three interlocking definitions: dianoia (thought) as the soul's silent inner dialogue with itself, doxa (opinion) as the completion of dianoia, and phantasia (appearance) as the mixture of perception and doxa."
textual_basis: The Visitor secures Theaetetus's assent to each term. For dianoia he asks what else it could be called when it occurs silently in the soul apart from opinion (264a). He then asks Theaetetus to name the same affection arriving through perception, securing phantasia (264a). Finally he states that dianoia is the soul's dialogue with itself, doxa is the completion of dianoia, and phantasia is a mixture of perception and doxa (264a-264b).
limits: This observation records the definitions as textually proposed and assented to. It does not evaluate whether these definitions are consistent with usage elsewhere in the dialogue or in other Platonic works.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0262

greek_terms:
  - μίμησις
  - ποίησίς
  - εἰδώλων
  - αὐτῶν ἑκάστων
  - ποιητικῆς
  - θεῖον
  - ἀνθρώπινον
english_gloss: imitation, production, images, the things themselves, productive art, divine, human
observation: "The Eleatic Stranger divides the productive art first into two parts: the divine and the human. This occurs at 265b as a preliminary step within the larger diairetic hunting of the sophist."
textual_basis: At 265b the Stranger states that of the productive art first let there be two parts, and specifies the divine and the human. Theaetetus asks which two, indicating he does not yet understand the division. The division is explicitly a first (proton) step within a larger diairetic procedure.
limits: The observation registers only the text's explicit division of productive art into two parts. It does not claim that this division is exhaustive or that it resolves the sophist definition. The larger context of the definitional hunt for the sophist is not addressed here.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0263
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 265b-265c

  text_sha256: bfe99954fdae9b1632d91a47b18b1be301704185615af1efe98959d89cbe09ed
greek_terms:
  - ζῷα δὴ πάντα θνητά
  - φυτὰ
  - ἄψυχα
  - θεοῦ δημιουργοῦντος
english_gloss: all mortal animals, plants, lifeless things, god as craftsman
observation: The Stranger maps the origin of all mortal animals, plants, and lifeless bodies to a divine craftsman as their cause. At 265c he contrasts this with the alternative that nature produces them spontaneously without intelligence.
textual_basis: The Stranger asks at 265c whether anything other than a god crafting brings these entities into being. He explicitly sets this against the view of the many that nature generates them from an automatic cause without thought.
limits: This observation records the mapping of a broad natural domain onto divine craft production as a specific analogy within the division. It does not assess whether the Stranger endorses the divine-craftsman view; the passage frames it as a question.
review_status: rejected
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0264

greek_terms:
  - μίμησις
  - εἰδώλων
  - ποιητικῆς
  - δύναμιν
  - αἰτία
english_gloss: imitation, images, productive art, power, cause
observation: The Stranger recapitulates the earlier definition of the whole of productive art as the power that causes things not previously existing to come into being afterward. At 265b-265c he obtains Theaetetus's assent to this recalled definition before applying it to the division of animal, plant, and lifeless generation.
textual_basis: At 265b the Stranger recalls the earlier definition of productive art as every power that becomes the cause for things not previously being to come to be later. Theaetetus responds that they remember (memnethemetha), confirming recollection of the prior agreement. This prior agreement about the definition is then used as the basis for asking whether mortal things come from a divine craftsman.
limits: The observation notes the recall-and-assent structure for a previously established definition. It does not evaluate whether the definition is correct or whether Theaetetus's memory is reliable. The observation is limited to the formal move of securing assent to an earlier definition before deploying it.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Eleatic Stranger marks a procedural turn by announcing that because the mimetic art (mimetike techne) has now encompassed the sophist, the productive art (poietike) must first be divided dichotomously (dicha diaireteon proten).
textual_basis: "At 264e-265a the Stranger signals a methodological correction: the earlier division of acquisition and production is bypassed because the sophist has been found under mimetic, which belongs to productive. He therefore declares a fresh division beginning with the productive art itself."
limits: This observation records the explicit argument-transition marker and methodological correction. It does not assess whether the division strategy is correct or complete.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

english_gloss: ""
observation: The Eleatic Stranger declares that the productive art must be split dichotomously (dicha diaireteon) as the next division step, because the sophist has now been located within the mimetic art, which itself falls under the productive art.
textual_basis: At 265a the Stranger says that since the mimetic art has encompassed the sophist, it is clear that the productive art itself must first be divided dichotomously (auten ten poietiken dicha diaireteon proten). This launches a new diairetic sequence.
limits: This observation records the announcement of a diairetic step, not the execution of any division that follows in subsequent Stephanus pages.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0267
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 264e-265a

greek_terms:
  - κατὰ τοὐπὶ δεξιὰ
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The Eleatic Stranger restates the division procedure: to proceed always by the right-hand side of what has been cut, clinging to the sophist's community (koinonia), until all common features are stripped away and his proper nature is displayed."
textual_basis: At 264e the Stranger says one should proceed according to the right-hand part of what has been divided (kata toupi dexia aei meros tou tmethentos), holding to the sophist's community until stripping away all common features and leaving his proper nature (oikeian phusin). This recapitulates and renews the diairetic method before the new division at 265a.
limits: This observation records the restated procedure. It does not evaluate the method's epistemic standing or completeness.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0268
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 263d-263e

greek_terms:
  - διάνοια
  - λόγος
  - διάλογος
  - ῥεῦμα
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Stranger defines thought (dianoia) as the soul's silent inner dialogue with itself without voice, and speech (logos) as the stream flowing from thought through the mouth with vocal sound, equating the two while distinguishing them by the inner/outer and silent/voiced contrast (263e).
textual_basis: At 263e the Stranger states that dianoia and logos are the same, except that dianoia is the inner dialogue of the soul with itself occurring without voice, while logos is the stream from dianoia going through the mouth with sound.
limits: This observation records the paired definition as proposed by the Stranger and assented to by Theaetetus; it does not evaluate whether this identification of thought with internalized speech holds across other Platonic dialogues.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0269

observation: The Stranger identifies affirmation (phasis) and negation (apophasis) as the two components present in speeches, presenting them as an opposed pair (263e).
textual_basis: At 263e the Stranger states that in speeches there is affirmation and negation, and Theaetetus confirms he knows this.
limits: This observation records the binary pairing of affirmation and negation as constituents of logos; it does not claim that the Stranger provides a full definition of either term, only that they are identified as an opposed pair within speeches.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0270
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 262c-262d

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt

greek_terms:
  - συμπλοκή
  - ὀνόμασι
  - ῥήματα
  - κεράσῃ
english_gloss: ""
observation: |
  The Eleatic Stranger proposes that a logos (statement) arises only when names (onomata) and verbs (rhemata) are mixed together; sequences of names alone or verbs alone do not constitute a logos.
textual_basis: |
  At 262c, the Stranger states that no logos has ever been constituted by a mere continuous sequence of names or verbs, and that such vocalizations reveal no action, inaction, or being until one mixes verbs with names. The first interweaving (symploke) immediately produces a logos.
limits: |
  The passage defines logos only in terms of its minimal syntactic structure; it does not address truth or falsity of logoi here.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
  At 262c, the Stranger asks Theaetetus whether he says that "anthropos manthanei" is the smallest and first logos, and Theaetetus agrees at 262d.
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0272
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 262c-262d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 262c-262d
  start_marker: 262c

greek_terms:
  - περαίνει
  - λέγειν
  - ὀνομάζειν
english_gloss: ""
observation: |
  The Stranger distinguishes logos from mere naming: a logos "accomplishes something" (ti perainei) by revealing something about things that are, come to be, have come to be, or will be, whereas mere naming does not.
textual_basis: |
  At 262d, the Stranger says that the interweaving of verbs with names reveals something about things that are or come to be or have come to be or will be, and does not only name but accomplishes something — and for this reason they call it legein (saying) and not merely onomazein (naming).
limits: |
  The distinction between saying and naming is presented as a consequence of the noun-verb interweaving definition; it is not independently argued for.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
  text_sha256: 4205a5414b6c2cf4daf2138bf41f37e860758fc3552c36cd4df85560c2437ca4
greek_terms:
  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Eleatic Stranger explicitly recalls the earlier dihairetic division of image-making into eikastike and phantastike at 264c, then recapitulates the aporia about where to place the sophist, and resumes the dihairetic procedure at 264d.
textual_basis: At 264c the Stranger says 'let us recall the divisions by forms made earlier' and at 264d says 'again let us attempt, splitting in two' the proposed genus, explicitly signaling a procedural restart after traversing the intervening arguments about falsehood, speech, and opinion.
limits: This observation notes the recapitulation and restart marker; it does not evaluate whether the resumed division succeeds or whether the intervening arguments resolved the earlier aporia.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0275
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 264c-264d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt

  start_marker: 264c

  end_char: 96394
  text_sha256: 4205a5414b6c2cf4daf2138bf41f37e860758fc3552c36cd4df85560c2437ca4
greek_terms:
  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: "At 264c-d the Stranger marks a transition: now that false speech and false opinion have been shown to exist, it follows that imitations of beings and a deceptive art from this disposition are possible, and that the earlier agreement about the sophist belonging to one of these two divisions stands."
textual_basis: The Stranger at 264c-d uses 'now that speech has appeared and false opinion has appeared' as a pivot, concluding that 'there can be imitations of beings and a deceptive art arising from this disposition,' then confirms the prior agreement about the sophist, bridging the intervening arguments about non-being and falsehood back to the dihairetic hunt.
limits: This records the transition marker and its argumentative function; it does not evaluate whether the inference from false speech to the possibility of deceptive art is valid.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0276
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 266c-266d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 266c-266d
  start_marker: 266c
  end_marker: 266d
  start_char: 99472
  end_char: 100382
  text_sha256: bf593fb325ae017dfdc1930f1a4f45a45c0153a7d5f59ba003042f88dcd6fff3
greek_terms:
  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: "At 266c-266d the Eleatic Stranger and Theaetetus establish a double two-way division of production: divine versus human production, and within each, the original thing versus its image. The human demiurgic art is divided into 'itself' (the thing produced by building) and a second kind like a dream made for waking people (produced by painting). The categories are then named by coined terms at 266d."
textual_basis: "At 266c the Stranger introduces the twofold nature of divine production: the thing itself and the accompanying image. At 266c he then applies the same division to human art, distinguishing a house made by building from a kind of dream-image for waking people made by painting. Theaetetus affirms (266d), and two technical neologisms are introduced at 266d for the pair."
limits: This observation records the formal division structure at 266c-266d. It does not assess whether the neologisms stand in the text or are intended to be suppressed. It does not evaluate whether the dream-image analogy for painting is adequate.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0277
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 266c-266d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt

  text_sha256: bf593fb325ae017dfdc1930f1a4f45a45c0153a7d5f59ba003042f88dcd6fff3
greek_terms:
  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: At 266c the Stranger describes the image produced by painting as 'like a dream for waking people' (hoion onar anthropinon egregorosin apergasmenen), applying a dream simile to characterize the ontological status of a painted image as a human-made likeness.
textual_basis: The phrase at 266c explicitly compares the product of painting to a dream. The simile uses the dream-waking contrast to mark the image's ontological difference from the real thing. Theaetetus assents without qualification at 266d.
limits: This observation records the simile usage at this specific span. It does not analyze the wider dream-appearance metaphor across Plato. It does not claim that the simile is original to this passage.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0278
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 266c-266d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 266c-266d
  start_marker: 266c
  end_marker: 266d
  start_char: 99472
  end_char: 100382
  text_sha256: bf593fb325ae017dfdc1930f1a4f45a45c0153a7d5f59ba003042f88dcd6fff3
greek_terms:
  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: "At 266d two operational terms are introduced by the Stranger for the two kinds of human production: 'self-working' (autourgike) for making the thing itself and 'image-making' (eidolopoiike) for making a likeness. The terms are coined to name the two branches produced by the division at 266c-266d."
textual_basis: At 266d the Stranger says one branch is labeled autourgike (making the self, text shows editorial deletion marks but the term is named), the other eidolopoiike (making images, also marked for deletion). Theaetetus responds at 266d by accepting the twofold division into divine and human and then within each the being and the generation of likenesses.
limits: This observation records the naming practice within the division. It does not resolve editorial questions about whether the terms are authorial or glossed. It does not claim that every Platonic division uses such neologisms.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0279
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 266a-266b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 266a-266b
  start_marker: 266a
  end_marker: 266b
  start_char: 98629
  end_char: 99472
  text_sha256: 7f3bdf7e5cf5bb614b2e0196be773d7fe5c800e1cfae622fd9c2130e10147cb9
greek_terms:
  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The Eleatic Stranger continues the diairetic method on the productive art (poietike), cutting it breadthwise (kata platos) and lengthwise (kata mekos) to produce four parts: two human and two divine. Each of these halves is then further divided into an autopoietic (self-productive) part and an eidolopoietic (image-making) part, and the productive art is again divided dichotomously."
textual_basis: At 266a the Stranger explicitly references the prior breadthwise cut and now applies a lengthwise cut, producing four parts. He then states that each half contains an autopoietic portion and an eidolopoietic portion, and that 'according to these the productive art is again divided in two' (kata tauta de palin he poietike diche diaireitai).
limits: This observation records one step in a long diairetic chain; it does not assess the validity of the division criteria or whether the fourfold partition is exhaustive.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
limits: This is an example enumeration inside the division; it is not a dream-report narrative or myth boundary.

```

```yaml

  - τινὲ
  - τινὲς
  - ἑνὸς
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Eleatic Stranger argues that saying 'something' cannot be isolated from what is, since the indefinite term marks one, two, or many; therefore anyone saying something says at least one thing.
textual_basis: At 237d, the Stranger distinguishes singular, dual, and plural indefinite terms and concludes that saying something involves saying one thing at minimum.
limits: This records the local predication and number distinction. It does not yet include the later resolution of the not-being problem.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0282

greek_terms:
  - μὴ τὶ
  - μηδὲν
  - μὴ ὂν
  - ἀπορίας
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Stranger concludes that one who does not say something says nothing at all, and that attempting to utter not-being should not be counted as speaking; Theaetetus calls this an endpoint of aporia.
textual_basis: At 237e, the Stranger moves from not saying something to saying nothing, then denies that one attempting to utter not-being should be described as speaking. Theaetetus answers that the argument has come to a complete aporia.
limits: This records the local speech constraint and reported aporia. It does not apply the constraint beyond the stage of argument reached here.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0283

  - μὴ ὄντι
```
