```yaml
  - ἀπορίας
english_gloss: ""
observation: Theaetetus recalls that false speech and opinion force the argument to attach being to not-being despite the prior impossibility claim, and the Stranger notes that classifying the sophist among false-workers and charmers opens many objections.
textual_basis: At 241b, Theaetetus identifies the pressure from false speech and false opinion, while the Stranger says that placing the sophist among false-workers and charmers exposes the account to many aporiai.
limits: The marker begins in a continuing exchange. This observation records the forced concession and resulting difficulty, not the later solution.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0284

  - ἐπιλαβέσθαι
  - παρασπασώμεθα
  - ἰσχυροῦ λόγου
english_gloss: ""
observation: Theaetetus says the sophist would be impossible to catch if the aporiai stand, but refuses to withdraw if they can seize even a little; the Stranger asks whether a small gain from a strong argument will suffice.
textual_basis: At 241c, Theaetetus uses capture language for the inquiry and says he would be ashamed to withdraw if any grip is possible. The Stranger converts this into a procedural question about being satisfied with a small gain from a powerful argument.
limits: This records the inquiry-as-capture language and renewed procedural consent. It does not identify the sophist anew.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

observation: The Stranger continues the mythic catalogue of earlier ontologies by attributing to later Muses a compromise account in which being is both many and one and is held together by enmity and friendship.
textual_basis: At 242e, the Stranger names Muses and describes a view combining plurality and unity through enmity and friendship.
limits: This records the text's mythic presentation of earlier ontologies. It does not assign the account to a historical school beyond the passage's own labels.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0286

  - τῶν πολλῶν
  - ἐπακολουθοῦμεν
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Stranger declines to censure famous older thinkers over whether their claims are true, but says they disregarded the many listeners and did not care whether the audience followed their accounts.
textual_basis: At 243a, the Stranger refuses to judge the truth of the old thinkers' claims, then safely reports that they neglected whether ordinary listeners followed their discourse.
limits: This records the audience-neglect remark inside the prior-ontology survey. It does not evaluate the historical thinkers independently.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0287
source_work: Sophist

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt

greek_terms:
  - ἕν
  - δυοῖν ὀνόμασιν
  - ὑπόθεσιν
  - καταγέλαστόν
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Stranger presses the monist hypothesis by asking whether 'one' and 'being' are two names for the same thing, then says the one-only thesis makes answering any such question difficult and makes acknowledging two names laughable.
textual_basis: At 244c, the Stranger tests whether the one and being are named by two names, and turns the presence of two names into pressure on the hypothesis that only one is.
limits: This records the naming pressure on the monist premise. It does not present a general theory of names.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0288
source_work: Sophist

greek_terms:
  - ὄνομα
  - πράγματος
  - ἕτερον
  - ταὐτόν
  - ὅλον
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The Stranger extends the naming test: if a name is other than the thing, two items are admitted; if the name is identical with it, the name becomes either of nothing or only a name of a name."
textual_basis: At 244d, the Stranger asks whether the name is different from the thing or the same as it. Each option generates difficulty for strict monism, and the segment ends by turning toward whether the whole differs from the one being.
limits: This records the name-thing dilemma in the monist test. The final whole/being question introduces the next step rather than completing it here.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0289

observation: The Stranger uses Parmenides' spherical description of being to argue that if being is a whole with middle and extremities, it necessarily has parts.
textual_basis: At 244e, the Stranger cites Parmenides and reasons from whole, middle, and extremities to the consequence that being has parts.
limits: This records the use of poetic authority and the part-whole inference. It does not quote the verse as independent evidence.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0290

  - ἓν
  - ἀμερὲς
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Stranger distinguishes having the property of unity across parts from being the one itself, since what is truly one must be entirely without parts.
textual_basis: At 245a, the Stranger says that something divided into parts and affected as one through all of them would be one in that way, but that the one itself must be without parts.
limits: This records the local mereological distinction between predicated unity and strict unity. It does not settle the broader account of being.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0291
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 245b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt

greek_terms:
  - χαλεπὴν
  - αἵρεσιν
  - πεπονθός
  - τῷ ἑνὶ
  - πλέονα
english_gloss: ""
observation: "The Stranger poses a hard choice: either being has unity as an affection and is one and whole in that way, or being must not be called whole; if it only has unity, it is not identical with the one and all things become more than one."
textual_basis: At 245b, the Stranger explicitly describes a difficult choice and follows the option of being affected by unity to the consequence that being and the one are not identical and the totality becomes more than one.
limits: This records the stated dilemma and consequence. It does not judge whether the dilemma is exhaustive.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0292
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 245c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt

greek_terms:
  - τὸ ὂν
  - τὸ ὅλον
  - ἐνδεὲς
  - οὐκ ὂν
  - πλείω
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Stranger derives the consequence that if being is not whole because it only has the affection of whole while the whole itself exists, being is deficient in itself and so will be not-being; separating being and whole also makes all things more than one.
textual_basis: At 245c, the Stranger reasons from being's relation to the whole to deficiency and not-being, then adds that if being and whole are separate, all things become more than one.
limits: This records the consequence internal to the Stranger's test of monism. It is not presented as a settled doctrine about being.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0293
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 245d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt

greek_terms:
  - οὐσίαν
  - γένεσιν
  - ὅλον
  - ἀπορίας
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Stranger extends the whole/being difficulty by arguing that neither being nor becoming should be named as real if the one or whole is not placed among beings, and that anything quantitative must be whole to that extent.
textual_basis: At 245d, the Stranger says that what has come to be has always come to be whole, and therefore one should not address being or becoming as real while excluding the one or whole from beings. He adds that even the smallest quantity must be whole to the extent that it is a quantity.
limits: This records the consequence in the monism test. It does not establish a general doctrine of whole and quantity outside the argument.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0294
source_work: Sophist

  - ἓν
  - δύο
  - πλάνην
  - ἱκανῶς
english_gloss: ""
observation: Theaetetus says each problem in the being inquiry attaches to another and produces a greater wandering than the previous claims, and the Stranger closes the survey of those who speak exactly about being and not-being as sufficient.
textual_basis: At 245e, Theaetetus comments that the currently emerging difficulties show a chain of greater and harder wandering. The Stranger then says they have not gone through all precise speakers about being and not-being, but the treatment should be enough before turning to others.
limits: This records the closure of one aporetic survey. It does not claim that every ontology has been examined.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0295

english_gloss: ""
observation: After the inquiry has made falsehood appear as really false and as one of the things that are, the Stranger says they can now count the relevant field into two undisputed forms.
textual_basis: At 266e, the Stranger recalls that falsehood has appeared as genuinely false and as something among beings, then asks whether they will now count the two forms without dispute; Theaetetus agrees.
limits: This records the transition from the falsehood argument back into division. It does not restate the whole proof that falsehood is.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0296
source_work: Sophist

  - ὀργάνων
  - φωνὴν
  - μίμησις
english_gloss: ""
observation: The Stranger divides appearance-making into products made through external instruments and products where the maker uses himself as the instrument, identifying the latter as imitation.
textual_basis: At 267a, the Stranger divides the phantastic kind in two, contrasts use of instruments with the maker providing himself as instrument, and illustrates this through bodily and vocal resemblance before naming it imitation.
limits: This records one diairetic step and its example. It does not yet classify the sophist within the resulting branch.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
textual_basis: At 267b, after assigning the self-instrumental appearance-making branch to imitation, the Stranger says imitators are split into those who know what they imitate and those who do not, and asks what greater division than knowledge and ignorance could be set down.
```

```yaml

  - φαίνεσθαι

observation: "The Stranger applies ignorant imitation to justice and virtue: many people do not know these things, but from opinion try to make themselves appear to possess them by imitating in deeds and speeches."
textual_basis: At 267c, the Stranger contrasts imitation of Theaetetus' visible shape with the shape of justice and virtue as a whole. He says many who are ignorant but have opinions strive to make what seems to them present in themselves appear, especially by imitating through actions and words.
limits: This records the passage's example of ignorant moral imitation. It does not assert that all imitation of virtue is ignorant.
```

```yaml
textual_basis: At 267d, the Stranger distinguishes the ignorant imitator from the knowing one, asks where fitting names for each can be found, and explains the difficulty by the old lack of attention to division of kinds by forms.
limits: This records a naming problem generated by diairetic work. It does not criticize all previous thinkers except as the passage states the neglect.
```

```yaml
textual_basis: At 267e, the Stranger names imitation with opinion and imitation with knowledge, then says the sophist was not among those who know but among imitators, so the opinion-imitator must be examined.
```

```yaml
textual_basis: At 268a, the Stranger distinguishes one figure who supposes he knows what he opines from another who, through rolling about in arguments, has suspicion and fear that he is ignorant of what he has shaped himself as knowing. He then proposes the terms simple imitator and ironic imitator.
limits: This records the contrast of two opinion-imitator types. It does not identify every ironic imitator as a sophist.
```

```yaml
textual_basis: At 268c, Theaetetus says the wise name is impossible since the figure has been placed as not knowing. Because he is an imitator of the wise, Theaetetus says he should take a related name and be called the truly actual sophist.
```

```yaml

observation: "The Stranger binds the final name backward through the completed divisions, locating the true sophist in the human, speech-bound, wonder-making part of image-making: an ironic, opinion-based, contradictory imitator."
textual_basis: At 268d, the Stranger composes the name from the end back to the beginning, listing the contrary-making, ironic, doxastic, mimetic branch of appearance-making, separated within human production and speech-bound wonder-making; Theaetetus fully assents.
limits: This records the dialogue's final diairetic formula for the sophist. It does not claim that all earlier partial definitions are thereby erased.
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0305
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 219c-219e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 219c-219e
  start_marker: 219c
  end_marker: 219e
  start_char: 6155
  end_char: 7241
  text_sha256: 30b8929cdf170ae8a3ae4304ee2a667658125579c5283643910957639a200255
greek_terms:
  - κτητικὴ
  - χειρωτικὸν
english_gloss: ""
observation: "At 219c-219e the Stranger performs a series of binary divisions to locate the art of angling: all arts split into acquisitive vs productive (219d), acquisitive into exchange vs coercive (219d), coercive into open/competitive vs hidden/hunting (219e), and hunting into inanimate-target vs animate-target (219e). Each cut is introduced by the Stranger as a dichotomy and confirmed by Theaetetus before the next cut proceeds."
textual_basis: The passage shows four successive two-way splits, each introduced with a division formula (whether to divide into two kinds) and each immediately accepted by Theaetetus, producing a nested classification tree that narrows toward angling.
limits: The observation records the division structure visible in the cited span only; it does not characterize the overall dihairetic method of the dialogue or compare this tree to later divisions targeting the sophist.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0306
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 219c-219e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt

  start_marker: 219c
  end_marker: 219e

  end_char: 7241
  text_sha256: 30b8929cdf170ae8a3ae4304ee2a667658125579c5283643910957639a200255
greek_terms:
  - ναί
  - δῆλον
  - φαίνεται
english_gloss: ""
observation: "Throughout 219c-219e Theaetetus responds to the Stranger's division proposals with brief affirmative tokens or one-word requests to continue, while the Stranger carries the argument in extended set speeches that pose each dichotomy. The assent density is high: nearly every turn by Theaetetus is a single-word or short-phrase confirmation."
textual_basis: Theaetetus's replies in this span consist of "yes," "clearly," "it appears so from what was said," "yes," and "in what way?" — all minimal — whereas the Stranger's turns propose full divisions with explanatory criteria.
limits: The observation describes the turn structure within this span only and does not claim a change in turn pattern elsewhere in the dialogue.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0307
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 219c-219e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 219c-219e
  start_marker: 219c
  end_marker: 219e
  start_char: 6155
  end_char: 7241
  text_sha256: 30b8929cdf170ae8a3ae4304ee2a667658125579c5283643910957639a200255
greek_terms:
  - κτητικὴ
  - δημιουργεῖ
english_gloss: ""
observation: "At 219c the Stranger states an explicit criterion for grouping several arts under the single genus of acquisitive art: these arts produce nothing but take hold of existing or already-produced things through words and actions. The produce-vs-acquire distinction is then used at 219d to separate acquisitive from productive arts as the first major division."
textual_basis: The Stranger says these arts create nothing but handle existing and already-generated things, some through words and actions, some by coercion, and therefore all could be called a single acquisitive art. Theaetetus agrees, and the Stranger immediately uses this criterion to ask under which of acquisitive and productive arts angling falls.
limits: The observation records the criterion as stated in this span; it does not trace how the criterion recurs or is modified in later divisions of the dialogue.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

observation: |
  At 223a the Stranger distinguishes a class that charges a fee in currency for
  association ostensibly undertaken for the sake of virtue, and Theaetetus
  agrees that this is the sophist they have been seeking, declaring they have
  found him and that this is the proper name.
textual_basis: |
  The Stranger describes the class that makes association on the pretext of
  virtue but takes money as payment; Theaetetus responds that they seem to have
  found the sophist and that calling him by this name would be appropriate
  (223a).
limits: |
  The observation records only that a definition is claimed as found at this
  point; it does not assess whether the definition is correct or final, since
  the search is subsequently reopened.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0309

greek_terms:
  - σοφιστική
  - δοξοπαιδευτικῆς
  - νομισματοπωλικῆς
english_gloss: ""
observation: |
  At 223b the Stranger recites the full nested chain of dichotomous divisions
  from acquisitive through hunting, land-animal, human, persuasion-based,
  private, wage-earning-removed, money-selling, opinion-molding hunting of
  rich young men, and concludes that the art should be called sophistic;
  Theaetetus assents completely.
textual_basis: |
  The Stranger strings together the successive division labels in a single
  genitive chain ending with the claim that, as the argument has turned out,
  the hunt in question should be called sophistic; Theaetetus responds with
  full agreement (223b). The passage includes editorial additions and
  deletions in the Greek text, indicating manuscript variance in the chain.
limits: |
  The observation records the chain as stated and the assent given; it does not
  evaluate the logical adequacy of each division or the manuscript variants.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0310
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 223c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 223c
  start_marker: 223c
  end_marker: 223c
  start_char: 13963
  end_char: 14477
  text_sha256: 500424634560f2687e2defa144e322a27db92ea947f185fef5069f6861c3134e
greek_terms:
  - φάντασμα
  - κτητικῆς
  - ἀλλακτικόν
english_gloss: ""
observation: |
  At 223c, immediately after the apparently complete definition, the Stranger
  says they should look further because the art sought is not simple but very
  complex, and because earlier stated definitions presented a phantom showing
  a different genus from what they now say it is. He then reopens the division
  by returning to the twofold acquisitive art and subdividing its exchange
  branch.
textual_basis: |
  The Stranger says the thing sought is no trivial part of art but very
  complex, and that earlier accounts gave a phantom that was not what they now
  say it is (223c). He then restarts the division from the acquisitive art as
  twofold into hunting and exchange, and further divides exchange into
  gift-giving and commerce (223c).
limits: |
  The observation records the reopening of the definition and the restart of
  division; it does not assess whether the new divisional path will succeed or
  how it relates to the prior chain.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0311
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 232c-232e

greek_terms:
  - τέχνη
english_gloss: ""
observation: "At 232c-232d the Stranger successively lists domains in which the sophist claims the ability to dispute: hidden divine matters, visible earthly and heavenly things, private discussions about being and generation, laws and all political matters, and the individual crafts. Each domain is introduced through a brief question-and-answer exchange in which Theaetetus confirms the sophist's claims."
textual_basis: The passage at 232c-232d moves through five domains in sequence, each introduced by the Stranger with a question and confirmed by Theaetetus with a brief assent, producing a ladder of escalating scope for the sophist's alleged competence.
limits: The observation records the enumeration structure and its confirmation pattern; it does not characterize the Stranger's attitude toward the sophist's claims or argue for a particular definition of the sophist.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

textual_basis: Theaetetus identifies the Stranger's description of publicly available counter-arguments against individual craftsmen as resembling the works of Protagoras concerning wrestling and other arts at 232d-232e, and the Stranger confirms there are many other such examples.
limits: The observation records the named reference to Protagoras and his published works; it does not assess Protagoras's historical role or compare this reference to other dialogues.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0313
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 232c-232e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt

greek_terms:
  []
english_gloss: ""
observation: At 232e the Stranger asks Theaetetus whether he thinks the sophist's universal art of contradiction is genuinely possible, and adds that the young may see this more sharply while the old see more dimly, disclaiming his own capacity to judge and deferring to his younger interlocutor.
textual_basis: After summarizing the sophist's art as a power of disputation about everything at 232e, the Stranger turns to Theaetetus with a direct question about its possibility and contrasts the sharper perception of the young with the dimmer sight of the old, including himself in the latter group.
limits: The observation records the self-disavowal and deferral move; it does not determine whether the Stranger's disavowal is sincere or ironic.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

observation: At 239b the Eleatic Stranger explicitly concedes that he himself has been worsted in the refutation concerning non-being and transfers the inquiry to Theaetetus, asking that they examine the question in Theaetetus rather than in himself.
textual_basis: The Stranger says at 239b that one would find him defeated both long ago and now concerning the refutation of non-being, and proposes that they no longer examine correct speech about non-being in him but instead investigate it in Theaetetus.
limits: The observation records the Stranger's self-described defeat and transfer of respondent; it does not determine whether the defeat is genuine or strategic.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0315

greek_terms:
  - οὐσία
  - τὸ ἓν
  - πλῆθος
english_gloss: ""
observation: At 239b-c the Stranger challenges Theaetetus to say something about non-being without adding being, the one, or multitude of number to it, setting an explicit constraint that makes the task appear impossible; Theaetetus declines at 239c, saying it would be strange to attempt when he sees the Stranger himself struggling.
textual_basis: The Stranger tells Theaetetus to try, without adding being, one, or number-multiplicity to non-being, to say something correct about it; Theaetetus responds that he would be eager but strange to attempt it seeing the Stranger suffering the same difficulties.
limits: The observation records the constraint and Theaetetus's refusal; it does not assess whether the constraint is genuinely impossible or dialectically motivated.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0316

greek_terms:
  - εἴδωλον
english_gloss: ""
observation: At 239c-d the Stranger anticipates that if they call the sophist's art phantastic or image-making, the sophist will counter-ask what an image is, and this opens a new definitional sub-question; Theaetetus at 239d responds with examples of images including water reflections, mirrors, paintings, and imprints.
textual_basis: The Stranger says at 239c-d that if they say the sophist has a phantastic art, he will easily turn the argument against them by asking what an image is when they call him an image-maker; he then tells Theaetetus to consider what the young man would answer, and Theaetetus offers reflections, mirrors, paintings, and imprints as examples.
limits: The observation records the opening of a new sub-question about images; it does not evaluate whether the sophist's counter-move is legitimate or whether Theaetetus's examples are adequate.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
greek_terms:
  - προσβολὴν
  - ἐπαφήν
  - σῶμα
  - οὐσίαν
english_gloss: Being defined by bodily contact or impact.
observation: At 246a-246b one party is described as holding that only what provides contact or impact is, and as defining body and being as the same.
textual_basis: The Stranger says this group drags everything down from heaven and the invisible, grasps stones and oaks, asserts that only what offers contact or impact is, and defines body and being as identical at 246a-246b.
limits: The record reports the position ascribed to this party in 246a-246b; it does not show that the dialogue has refuted or accepted the position within this segment.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
english_gloss: Intelligible incorporeal forms asserted as true being.
observation: At 246b-246c the opposing party is described as maintaining that intelligible and incorporeal forms are true being, while treating bodies as becoming rather than being.
textual_basis: The Stranger says the opponents defend from the invisible, force intelligible and incorporeal forms to be true being, and in arguments break up the bodies of the other side by calling them a kind of becoming instead of being at 246b-246c.
limits: The record identifies the contrast attributed to this party in 246b-246c; it does not establish the final account of being in the dialogue.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
  - εἴδεσιν
english_gloss: The other is counted as a fifth kind among the selected forms.
observation: At 255d-256a the Stranger explicitly adds the nature of the other as a fifth item among the forms under consideration.
textual_basis: The passage moves from distinguishing being and the other at 255d to the statement at 255e that the nature of the other must be spoken of as fifth among the forms being selected.
limits: This records the enumeration within the local argument; it does not settle how the complete set of forms is to be interpreted elsewhere.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

greek_terms:
  - διὰ πάντων
  - μετέχειν
  - ἰδέας
english_gloss: Each thing is other than the rest by participation in the idea of the other.
observation: At 255d-256a the Stranger explains the predication of being other by saying that the other runs through all the forms and that each is other than the rest through participation in the idea of the other.
textual_basis: "The claim at 255e states both the passage of the other through all the selected items and the reason each one is other than the others: not by its own nature, but by participation in the relevant idea."
limits: This observation is limited to the stated account of otherness in this passage and does not generalize every predicate in the dialogue to the same model.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
observation_id: obs_sophist_0322
source_work: Sophist
stephanus_span: 255d-256a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/sophist.txt
  stephanus_span: 255d-256a
  start_marker: 255d
  end_marker: 256a
  start_char: 77488
  end_char: 78745
  text_sha256: 6fd61e3c89fe4e8458f32e2e50f7940d7d74298a8b116d3976a3746e3c4bd709
greek_terms:
  - κίνησις
  - στάσεως
  - ταὐτόν
  - μὴ ταὐτόν
english_gloss: Motion is said to be other than rest and both same and not same in different respects.
observation: At 255d-256a the Stranger applies the account of otherness to motion, first denying that motion is rest while affirming that it is, and then saying that motion must be agreed to be both same and not same without distress because the statements are not made in the same way.
textual_basis: The sequence at 255e-256a treats motion as other than rest, denies that it is rest, affirms it through participation in being, then treats motion as other than the same and explains the paired affirmation and denial by distinguishing how the terms are said.
limits: The record notes the local procedural handling of an apparent contradiction; it does not infer a broader doctrine beyond this segment.
review_status: accepted
```
